(2) **`V2033 opaque_not_schema`** (TYPE): "`Query<Row>` is opaque outside `std/sql`, so it is not `Schema`: a value comes from its module's constructors (`sql"…"`), not from data"; when the opaque type is a field of the required type the note names the path (`Report.q: Query<Row>`). Its own code, as `V2031` is, because the fix is specific and an agent reads the code before the message. (3) **No decoder opt-in in v0.** A program that has the representation in data decodes the representation and calls the module's checked constructor, one visible line at the boundary: `Html.text(s)` or `Html.untrusted(u)` (and `Html.raw(s, @trust("…"))` for stored markup, which `sakura audit` lists), `UrlPart.from(s)`, `Uuid.parse(s)`, `Decimal.parse(s)`. `UrlPart` does not "decode by encoding": a decoder that transforms the data it is given hides the boundary, and `from` is the same one call in the open. (4) **Std.** `Query<R>`, `Html`, `UrlPart`, `Prompt`, `File`, `Cassette`, `Tool<e>`, `Router<e>` stay opaque and have no JSON form outside their modules. `Uuid` becomes `pub type Uuid = String where is_canonical(it)` (as `Path` did, §17.54): `parse` lowercases then validates, `from_canonical` is the generated `from`, `to_string` stays as the coercion, `Random.uuid()` is unchanged, and `{ id: Uuid }` decodes with the check; `Show` prints the bare text. `Decimal` becomes `pub type DecimalRepr = { whole: Int, frac: List<Int> }` plus `pub type Decimal = DecimalRepr where is_canonical(it)` (`whole` the floor, `frac` digits 0–9 without a trailing zero): its JSON form is the record it already has today, so `examples/secrets`'s `Request.post_json(url, amount)` is unchanged; a predicate that leaves the contract subset falls to a `@total` helper. (5) **Audit and manifest: nothing new.** The rule is a compile error, so there is no site to list; a module decoding its own opaque type is its own construction and is read with its constructors. Why: §9 promised that a `Query` comes only from `sql"…"` and an `Html` only from escaping code; a constraint derived for free gave data a constructor the module never wrote. Rejected: splitting `Schema` into encode and decode constraints (every std signature changes, and the checker cannot tell which a bound is for); `V2032` with a note (the precedent of `V2031` is a code per explanation); a declared codec attribute now (speculative until a std type needs a form a refinement cannot express, §16.17; `Decimal` as `"12.34"` is the candidate); decoding an opaque type as `Untrusted<T>` (an `Untrusted<Html>` is still an `Html` after `trust`); a std generator `gen.html` or `gen.query` (code composes one with `gen.map(gen.string(8), Html.text)`). Performance: one visibility test per obligation on an opaque ADT; nothing at run time. Changed: §4.2, §4.3, §4.4, §7.4, §9, §10.3, §16.17. Impact: `sakura_types` (`adt_holds`, a `property` obligation), `sakura_diagnostics` (`V2033`), `sakura_cli` (`--contracts` skip), `std/uuid`, `std/decimal`, `std/INDEX.md`, `docs/llms.txt`, snapshots that pinned `Uuid("…")`; the security packet part C; routed to Opus as plan WS10 (gate 10 fix).

131. **A manifest may not name its package `std` or `main` (`V8002`; WS9 item 8 deviation 4; round 8).** Decision: `sakura.toml` with `name = "std"` or `name = "main"` is rejected when the manifest is read, `V8002 reserved_package_name` (PACKAGE phase), naming the file and the line: `std` is the standard library and `main` is the package of a file run without a manifest (§9, §17.127), and a user package under either name would make an `import std/…` or `import main/…` mean two things. Why: §17.127 made the manifest's name the first import segment, so the name space must stay unambiguous. Rejected: silently renaming (an import written against the manifest would then be wrong). Changed: §9 (one clause). Impact: `sakura_analysis` manifest loading, `sakura_diagnostics`, a `cli.rs` test and a `tests/ui` fixture; routed to WS10 item 6.

132. **`std/INDEX.md` and the website std listing follow the formatter's row order (WS9 deviation 6; round 8).** Question: the std reformat (§17.121 item 6) sorted effect rows as §12.5 states (alphabetical, `Fail<…>` last) in 35 files, and `std/INDEX.md` and `website/content/docs/stdlib.mdx` still show the hand-extracted order. Decision: the source is the truth and the formatter's order is the canonical one; **Sol regenerates** `std/INDEX.md`'s "Signatures" section and the website's std signatures from the formatted source, by hand as the INDEX header says, until `sakura doc --index` exists (then the index is generated and gate 5 diffs it). Changed: none. Impact: `std/INDEX.md`, `website/content/docs/stdlib.mdx`; routed to Sol.

133. **`sakura fmt`: an `if` with an `else` breaks every branch or none (std reformat observation; round 8).** Question: the formatter leaves `if String.contains(unreserved, c) { out = out + c } else {` with a multi-line `else` (`std/urlpart.sakura`), so one branch is inline and the other a block, and the condition and the first statement read as one line. Decision: §12.5's "single-statement blocks stay on one line when they fit" applies to an `if` only when **every** branch of that `if` fits on one line (an `else if` chain counts as one `if`); otherwise each branch is a block with its statements on their own lines, `else {` and `} else if … {` on the `}` line as today. Why: the rustfmt rule; a reader scans branch heads, and a mixed layout hides one. Rejected: a width-only rule (the mixed form fits within 100 columns and is still the problem). Changed: §12.5. Impact: `sakura_fmt` (one layout rule), its corpus and `props`; a std reformat of the files it changes, with no hand edits (`fmt_clean` stays green); routed to WS10 item 7.

134. **`List.set` on the JS backend stays a path copy; in-place update when unique is the native reuse path (WS-B sieve, 34×/91× after `7ced139`; round 8).** Decision: deferred to the performance round with the measure in place. §17.126 stands: the JS backend has no reference counts, so "provably unique" there would be a static last-use analysis, which is M4-OPT/M5 territory (§17.109's reuse hit rate, §17.118's paired unique/shared workloads); the matched `sieve` ratio is recorded in `bench/README.md` as exactly the gap the native in-place path must close, and no JS special case is added before that path exists. Changed: none. Impact: `bench/README.md` notes the ratio as the §17.126 measure; nothing else. Amended by §17.149: ownership facts computed in Core authorise in-place update on the JS backend too, with the persistent operation as the fallback.
135. **A field assignment on a `var` of a refined type is `V2034`; rebuild through `T.from` or `T.validate` (WS10 question, REQUESTS 2026-10-18; round 9).** Question: `type Pos = PosRepr where it.n > 0; fn f(p: Pos) -> Pos { var x = p; x.n = -1; x }` checks and returns a `Pos` with `n = -1`, and since §17.130 made `Decimal` a refinement, `var x = d; x.frac = [5, 0]` builds a non-canonical `Decimal` outside `std/decimal`, which `opaque` used to forbid. Analysis: §8.1 says `x.n = v` on `var x` is sugar for `x = PosRepr { ..x, n: v }`, and a record update yields the base type, so the desugared statement assigns a `PosRepr` to a `var` of type `Pos`, which no other spelling of the program may do; the checker's `Stmt::Assign` arm (`check/expr.rs`) walks the field path and checks the value against the last field's type without looking at the types on the path. The hole is in the sugar, not in refinements. Decision: (1) **Rule.** In `x.f1.….fn = v`, when the type of `x` or of any proper prefix `x.f1.….fk` is a refined type (§4.3), the statement is rejected with **`V2034 refined_field_assignment`** (TYPE), naming the first refined prefix and its declaration: "`x.n = …` rebuilds `x` as a `PosRepr`, but `x` is a `Pos` (`PosRepr where it.n > 0`); a field assignment would skip the predicate". The rule holds in every module, the declaring one included: a refinement's invariant is its predicate, which `from` runs, so the declaring module has nothing to gain from a privilege (an opaque type's module owns the constructors, which is why §17.130 (1) privileges it). A refinement over a refinement is named at the outermost one and the rebuild nests (`Pos2.from(Pos.from(PosRepr { ..x, n: v }))`). Whole reassignment of a refined `var` is unchanged (`x = next(x)`; `d = Decimal.add(d, y)` in a loop), as is a field assignment on a plain record whose field is refined (`r.total = Decimal.add(r.total, y)`: the prefix `r` is a record and the value is checked against `Decimal` as today). (2) **Fix.** One `suggested` fix of impact `local` that rewrites the statement to `x = Pos.from(PosRepr { ..x, n: -1 })` (for a prefix, `r.total = Decimal.from(DecimalRepr { ..r.total, frac: [5, 0] })`). It is not `machine_applicable` because it adds a `from` site, a trusted introduction that `sakura audit` lists (§4.3); the message names `Pos.validate(…)?` as the spelling for data. (3) **Loops.** An accumulator that changes a field of a refined value per iteration pays one visible predicate per iteration through `from`, or keeps the base type inside the loop and enters the refinement once after it; both are the program's choice and neither is hidden. Why: §4.3's soundness argument is that a validated value cannot be invalidated later, and this sugar was the one statement that could; §17.103 forbids a hidden check at a use site, and re-checking silently at the assignment would be exactly that (the predicate's cost per assignment; `Decimal.is_canonical` walks `frac`), with a `D010` blamed on a statement that names no `from` and that no audit lists. Rejected: re-checking at the assignment (above); inferring `var x = p` at the base type (a `var` holding a refined value and reassigned whole is common and sound, and a silently widened `var` fails later where a `Pos` is expected); letting the sugar retype the `var` (a `var` has one type, §8.1); a `Pos { ..x, n: v }` update form that re-checks (it is `Pos.from(PosRepr { ..x, n: v })` with the audit site hidden); `V2001` with a note (the fix is specific, and `V2031`/`V2033` set the precedent of a code per explanation). Performance: none at run time; one type-definition lookup per field on the path at check time. Changed: §4.3, §8.1, `docs/llms.txt`. Impact: `sakura_types` (`check/expr.rs`, the `Stmt::Assign` arm resolves each prefix's type and stops at a refined one), `sakura_diagnostics` (`V2034` and its explain page), `tests/ui/V2034_refined_field_assignment.sakura` (WS10's `Pos` program) and `tests/ui/V2034_refined_prefix_assignment.sakura` (`r.total.frac = [5, 0]` through `Decimal`), `tests/semantics/refined_var_rebuild.sakura` (whole reassignment in a loop, the `from` rebuild, a refined field on a plain record, and an `expect-defect D010` rebuild that violates), `sakura test --std` and the examples unchanged (no std or example file assigns a field of a refined `var` today; `tests/semantics/records.sakura` assigns fields of plain records and stays green), one sentence in `website/content/docs/tour/types.mdx`; routed to **Opus as plan WS11**.
136. **Peak-RSS gate: the Node block is confirmed by a second group, the Bun verdict is advisory until the noise study clears it (WS-A measurement `99e1826`/`066a938`, WS-P1 deviation 7, WS-P3 deviation 6; round 9).** Question: §17.110 makes peak RSS up by more than 25% an immediate block on every lane. Astra measured unchanged `824a876` builds: for `shapes`/Sakura n=50000 the five-process maxima of three groups vary 2.2% on Node and 33.8% on Bun (individual Bun peaks 205–332 MB), individual peaks spread more than 25% in 16 of 168 Node cells and 9 of 168 Bun cells, and Astra kept the maximum-based block on both engines with medians printed as diagnostics. In the same window the gate caught a real regression: `binary_trees`/Node 198 → 291 MB after WS-P3 was a dead ANF temporary in direct twins (§17.138), not noise. Decision: the gate stays maximum-based and stays a block, with two amendments. (1) **Confirmation, as for time.** On Node a maximum rise above 25% in a cell is confirmed by one automatic second group of five fresh processes of that cell in the same job (the mechanism §17.110 already prescribes for the 10% time block); the block needs both groups' maxima above the baseline maximum by more than 25%, and a single crossing is a warning annotation carrying the `rss` diagnostic rows (base and head maxima, medians, counts). (2) **Bun is advisory.** On Bun the same crossing, confirmed or not, is a warning annotation and never a block, until Astra's unchanged-commit noise study (at least ten groups of five on the reference machine with load recorded, and on `macos-latest`, over every cell the §17.110 selection rule picks) shows that rule (1) would have produced no false block on Bun; that result amends this entry and turns the Bun block on. The emitted module and `runtime/js` are the same files on both engines, so a memory regression in the compiler or the runtime shows on Node, whose peaks are the stable measurement; Bun's peak is dominated by JSC's heap sizing under a changed allocation rate, which the unchanged-build swings (304–402 MB in WS-P1's runs) demonstrate. Baselines stay human-blessed (§17.110); the harness never reruns until green, and the confirmation group's samples are retained in the artefact like every other. Why: a block that fires on unchanged code is either ignored or rerun until quiet, and both end the gate; a block confirmed by an independent group on the engine whose measurement is stable keeps what caught `binary_trees`. Rejected: a wider Bun margin (a number from one cell); a median gate (Astra's evidence: the median also crosses on selected cells and hides a peak-only regression); more processes per cell everywhere (double cost for a tail statistic that converges slowly); dropping the gate (it found a real bug within a day); a quiet-host precondition (a CI runner has none; the reference machine's load is already recorded for the bless review). Performance: none at run time; one extra group per crossing in CI. Changed: §17.110 (pointer), plan `performance-r1.md` §3.4. Impact: `bench/harness` compare and the confirmation group (WS-B, Opus) with fixtures (a Node first-group-only crossing warns, a confirmed crossing blocks, a Bun crossing warns); `bench/README.md` "Peak RSS" section and the noise study (Astra); `bench.yml` only if the timing confirmation lives there (Sol).
137. **Z3 5.3.1 and cvc5 1.4.2 are installed in the required Rust CI jobs, and a skipped verify test in CI is a failure (round 9).** Question: `sakura verify`'s CLI tests (`verify_reports_the_scale_acceptance`, `verify_warns_w7002_on_a_vacuous_obligation`) and `sakura_verify`'s `tests/live.rs` skip with a message when the pinned Z3 is not on `PATH`, and no CI job installs it, so the §14.3 M4 acceptance program has never run in CI. Decision: (1) the existing `Rust (ubuntu-latest)` and `Rust (macos-latest)` jobs of `ci.yml` install the pinned solvers before `cargo test --workspace --locked`: the Z3 5.3.1 and cvc5 1.4.2 release archives for the runner's OS and architecture, downloaded from the projects' GitHub releases with their sha256 pinned in the workflow, unpacked outside the checkout and added to `GITHUB_PATH`, cached with `actions/cache` keyed on tool, version, OS and architecture; the versions are written once in the workflow's `env`, and the tests' pin check (`SolverKind::pinned`) is the cross-check, because a mismatch fails the tests with the solver's reported version. No separate job and no change to `ci-required`: the tests already live in the workspace run that `ci-required` needs; the point is that they stop being skippable. (2) `has_z3()` in `cli.rs` and `z3()` in `live.rs` **fail instead of skipping when the `CI` environment variable is set** (GitHub sets `CI=true`), so an install step that breaks cannot silently restore today's state; locally the skip and its message stay. (3) Both operating systems, because §17.106 requires statuses to be deterministic across machines and the two runners are the cheapest cross-machine check of that; a Z3 or cvc5 upgrade is a design entry first (§17.106) and a workflow edit second. cvc5 is included because M4-VERIFY's acceptance (plan §3.1) says a cross-check agrees on every `unsat` in the corpus and `live.rs` already performs it when cvc5 is present. Why: a test that never runs in CI is documentation; the acceptance of M4's verification half must execute on every pull request that can break it. Rejected: a separate non-required `verify` job (red without blocking is the state being left); nightly only (verification changes land through pull requests); building Z3 from source (minutes per run, and the release binaries are the pinned artefact); the compiler downloading a solver (plan §3.5: nothing is downloaded). Performance: compile and check latency unchanged; about a minute per job on a cache miss. Changed: none in the design text; plan §3.1 M4-TEST. Impact: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (Sol), the two guards in `crates/sakura_cli/tests/cli.rs` and `crates/sakura_verify/tests/live.rs` (Sol), a gate-report note that `W7002` is now exercised through the CLI in CI; acceptance: a master run whose logs show the three tests running on both runners, and a deliberate run with the install step disabled failing them.
138. **`binary_trees` peak RSS after WS-P3: a dead one-use ANF temporary in direct twins; fixed by adjacent producer–consumer inlining in direct emission, the gate unchanged (Astra `be5ec42`, WS-P3 deviation 6; round 9).** Finding (Astra): the direct `run$d` holds `r_1 = bottom_up$d(t_1)` (the stretch tree) live after its only use `r_2 = check$d(r_1)` until the frame returns, so 65,535 dead nodes (6.8 MB) survive the first scavenges, V8's allocation-site pretenuring then sizes the old generation for them, and the peak lands at 291 MB against 196 MB for CPS and 153 MB for hand JS; inlining the bind as `check$d(bottom_up$d(t_1))` brings five peaks to 154–165 MB at the same time, and last-use clearing (`r_1 = undefined`) does the same. Decision: **endorsed as routed to WS-P2**, the next owner of `emit.rs`. The mechanism is general, not a benchmark special case: in a direct twin, a one-use ANF bind whose value is a reference (not a scalar) and whose single use is an argument of the **immediately following** bind is emitted inline in that argument position. Adjacency is what makes it free: every other argument of the consumer is an atom (ANF), so evaluation order, the order of possible defects, blame spans, `Check` binds (never crossed: a `Check` between the two breaks adjacency) and the gauge prologue and epilogue are untouched. A one-use temporary that is not adjacent to its consumer is cleared at its last use (`r = undefined` after the consuming statement) only if WS-P2 measures a remaining regression once the inlining lands; it is not added speculatively. The CPS body is unchanged (its lifetimes are the frames'). The gate stays: no `--max-old-space-size`, no forced GC, no re-bless of the 198 MB baseline, no `binary_trees` exemption; the WS-P3 block closes when `bench compare` of the full unchanged configuration is green on the reference machine (Astra remeasures). Astra's second observation, that a `Tree` costs 104 bytes per node as a tagged record with a fields array against hand JS's 40, is a representation question for the LIR and the next performance round, not for this fix: Astra writes it up (`research/10-evidence/record-representation.md`: the JS object shapes, the LIR layout table's equivalent, what `Show`/`Eq`/`Schema` need from the representation) and a later round decides; nothing in the ABI changes now. Why: the lifetime fix removes a dead root the hand-written program never had and makes direct code no worse than CPS on memory, which is what §17.112 claimed; the representation change is a different decision with ABI cost. Performance: `binary_trees`/Node peak back under the baseline plus 25% with the WS-P3 time unchanged (40.5 against 40.2 ms in Astra's diagnostic build). Changed: none. Impact: `crates/sakura_codegen_js/src/emit.rs` (WS-P2, Opus); a `tests/semantics` fixture pinning blame and defect order across an inlined bind (`direct_inlined_bind_blame.sakura`: the producer defects, then a variant where the consumer defects, each `expect-defect` with the span the un-inlined form reported); an emitted-JS snapshot of `research/10-evidence/micro.sakura` showing the inlined form; the full-suite remeasure and a `bench/deltas.md` row (Astra). **Amended (round 11, §17.156 (4)):** the consumer must be a call, never an allocation (an allocation consumer kept `binary_trees` at 257–290 MB); the snapshot is the regression test's program, since `micro.sakura` has no adjacent one-use reference bind.
139. **After the in-flight work: M4 remainder, the performance close-out and M5 preparation, in that order (round 9).** Question: with M3 complete pending gate 10, M4's R1–R3, L1–L3, V1–V3 and C1 landed, W1/R4 landing and WS-P2, WS-P4 and WS-P6 in flight (WS-P5 after WS-P4), what runs next and in what order. Decision: the pace stays "develop properly toward a stable version", with no beta push; the order is (a) WS11 (§17.135) and the solver CI change (§17.137) now, independent of everything; (b) W2, the whole corpus and the twelve examples on WASM with identical traces and host-import coverage, as soon as W1 is on master, with M4-TEST's parity matrix in `ci.yml`; (c) L4, the LIR's direct facts agreeing with `--dump direct` (§17.118), direct calls and frame reuse in direct regions and unboxed scalars at internal boundaries, after WS-P2 has settled `direct.rs`; (d) the performance round close-out when P2, P4, P5 and P6 are in (full-suite remeasure, the §17.109 JS acceptance, an owner-blessed baseline, the §17.136 harness change); (e) M4-OPT (`--layout=niche`, the proved-check removal deleting the predicate's computation, the 2× all-frame/all-box bar on WASM) after W2's parity; (f) the WASM lane in `bench` and the §17.109 M4 envelope; (g) M5 preparation as research only (Perceus reuse over the LIR's `Dup`/`Drop`, a Cranelift survey, the record representation of §17.138, `W8001`), feeding a design round that writes the M5 plan. Gate 10 is the owner's and lifts the §17.128 freeze whenever it is signed. Why: parity before optimisation (M4-OPT measures against a corpus that must already agree on both backends), measurement before M5 (the M4 envelope is M5's baseline), and nothing starts on a surface another in-flight workstream is editing. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §2 (WS11), §3.1 (M4-TEST), §3.7 (new). Impact: the plan routes each item with owner, paths, dependencies and acceptance.

140. **The agent DX programme: an agent must be able to learn, write, check and ship Sakura from the toolchain alone (owner request 2026-10-18; research/11; round 10).** Question: the owner asks for "proper skills and stuff, in the website etc, so agents can easily understand the language, and whatever else is needed". Decision: a programme of nine workstreams, routed by `docs/plans/agent-dx.md` (DX0–DX8), in this order: DX0 correct every agent-facing surface that contradicts the built compiler (§17.141); DX1 one portable Sakura skill generated from the compiler's own sources and tested in CI (§17.142); DX2 `sakura new`/`init` write agent instructions and `sakura setup` installs client adapters (§17.143); DX3 generated error-code reference pages (§17.144); DX4 a tested cookbook (§17.145); DX5 the agent evaluation pilot, offline parts in CI, paid runs owner-approved (§17.146); DX6 the first release and installers, prepared and awaiting owner authorisation (§17.147); DX7 editor support; DX8 playground and Linguist, deferred behind their prerequisites (§17.148). Principles that every workstream obeys: (1) **one source, mechanical adapters**: the language guide is `docs/llms.txt`, the std surface is `compiler/std/INDEX.md` and the std sources, the diagnostics are `sakura_diagnostics::codes::REGISTRY`, the examples are `compiler/examples/`; the skill, the error pages, the cookbook and the website mirrors are generated from them by one generator and CI fails on a stale regeneration; nothing agent-facing is hand-maintained twice. (2) **Compiler-matched**: every bundle states the commit and compiler version it was generated from, and the CLI embeds its own copy (as it embeds `docs/llms.txt`), so an agent with the binary has the matching skill without the network. (3) **Executable**: every complete Sakura code block that an agent may copy is a fixture the CLI harness checks, and fragments are labelled; no advertised sample is skipped. (4) **Nothing publishes or escalates by itself**: scaffolding writes only project-local files, never global dotfiles; no workflow publishes a binary, package, extension or plugin without the owner's explicit run; paid model calls need an approved budget. (5) **Measured, not assumed**: the pilot measures discovery and activation of the skill, not only its content, because research/11 §2 shows a skill that is not invoked is a cost without a benefit. Why: a language for agents is adopted through the agent's first ten minutes, and research/11 §1 found that today those minutes meet stale status claims, no skill, no scaffolded instructions and no release. Rejected: a second language reference beside `docs/llms.txt` (two references drift; the skill links the guide and adds the workflow, the pitfalls and the repair table); a Sakura-specific MCP docs server (the compiler MCP's `docs_query` already ranks the guide, the std index and the registry); fine-tuning or training data before the docs-and-tools loop is measured (research/11 §5). Performance: none at run time; the generator runs in CI. Changed: §14.3 gains the programme's pointer. Impact: Opus codes (generator, CLI, harness, eval runner, release workflow, VS Code client), Sol does the mechanical parts (mirrors, adapters, CI wiring, website routes, ports), Astra authors prose and designs the experiment; the plan names paths, dependencies and acceptance per workstream.

141. **DX0: the agent-facing surfaces say what the compiler does today, before anything else is built (research/11 §1, §6 "very high / S"; round 10).** Decision: the following are fixed first, each by reading the source, never by guessing: (1) MCP `compiler_info.features` advertises `backends: ["js"]` and `verify: false` while `sakura build --target wasm` and `sakura verify` exist; it reports what the running binary provides, derived from the same facts `sakura --help` uses (a `backends` list, `verify: true` with the solver status it finds, `test_execution`), and `docs/mcp.md` and `website/content/docs/agents/mcp.mdx` stop calling the transport, options and `compiler_info` fields unsettled; `frozen_specs` is documented as optional on `check_patch`/`apply_patch`, and the design's `--tools` export and HTTP transport (§11.2) are labelled **planned** wherever the pages mention them. (2) `docs/llms.txt`'s status section still says the performance work is planned and WASM is M4-future; it states what landed (direct code, `--dump direct`, `--no-direct`, `--target wasm`, proved arithmetic) and what did not. (3) Root `AGENTS.md` says no workspace or toolchain exists; it points at `compiler/rust-toolchain.toml` and the three workspace checks as checks that *do* run today. (4) `website/lib/source.ts`'s llms preamble says the M3 std is not built; the llms-txt page calls `prompt` both implemented and future and `doc --search` planned; the diagnostics page says no phase-7 code is named while the verify page documents `W7002`; `STABILITY.md` is re-read against the same list. Each fix is one sentence of truth, not a rewrite. Acceptance: an `mcp.rs` test asserts `compiler_info` against the build's actual capabilities (a build with the WASM backend compiled in reports it); a doc test greps the agent pages for "planned"/"future"/"not yet" beside a feature the CLI test suite exercises; website `fmt:check`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `build` on the touched files; every local link checked. Why: research/11 §1 calls the contradiction an onboarding defect, and an agent that reads `verify: false` never runs `sakura verify`. Rejected: waiting for the skill to carry the corrections (the skill links these pages). Performance: none. Changed: none in this document (§11.2's `--tools`/HTTP stays as design, labelled planned by the pages). Impact: Astra audits and writes the sentences, Sol applies them, Opus changes `compiler_info` (`sakura_mcp/src/lib.rs`); plan DX0.

142. **DX1: one portable `sakura` skill, generated from the compiler's sources, embedded in the CLI, mirrored on the website (research/11 §2–§3; round 10).** Decision: **Format.** Agent Skills `SKILL.md` (YAML frontmatter `name: sakura`, a `description` that names the triggers: `.sakura` files, `sakura.toml`, effects, holes, Sakura diagnostics; `license`; `compatibility` naming the CLI; `metadata` with `version`, `sakura-compiler-version`, `sakura-compiler-git` and `sakura-edition`), a body under 500 lines and about 2,000 tokens, and `references/` loaded on demand; no `scripts/`, no `allowed-tools`, no hooks (the CLI already is the deterministic tool; a guide must not grant anything by loading). **Content of the body**, in this order: identify the compiler (`sakura --version`, `sakura prompt`); the one-screen cheat sheet against TypeScript/Rust transfer (explicit signatures, `!` rows and their omission for pure functions, positional calls, binding annotations instead of call-site generics, explicit non-prelude imports, `var` rebinding for `List.set`/`Map.set`, the `n == 0` guard idiom); the numbered loop (retrieve signatures with `sakura doc`/`symbol_query` → write signatures and holes → `check --json` → fill holes → `test --json` → `build` → report), with the CLI fallback when MCP is absent; four short complete examples (`Fail` recovery, a local handler, a refinement entry check, a deterministic fake effect); the error→cause→repair table seeded from `tests/ui` and extended from the pilot's actual failures (`V3001` local handler versus widening, `V2050` unfinished code, `V8020` change the implementation not the frozen task); the completion rule (no holes, independent tests pass, no contract or permission widened, audit/verify reported as evidence, never as certification). **References** are generated: `references/language.md` is `docs/llms.txt` verbatim; `references/stdlib.md` from the std sources through the `doc` machinery (indexed by module and task, full signatures; the hand-regenerated `std/INDEX.md` is replaced by this generator's output, closing its own "until `sakura doc --index` exists" note); `references/diagnostics.md` from the registry; `references/recipes.md` from DX4's recipes; `assets/` carries the DX2 templates. **Source and generator.** The authored body lives at `skills/sakura/SKILL.md` (a new top-level directory, recorded as an amendment to ADR 0002); a workspace tool `compiler/tools/agent_docs` (beside `tools/sakura_eval`) links `sakura_diagnostics` and the std loader and writes the references, the DX3 pages, the DX4 pages and the website mirror deterministically; `cargo run -p agent_docs -- --check` fails CI when any output is stale. The CLI embeds the bundle (every file as a string, like `LLMS_TXT`), and `sakura setup` (§17.143) writes it; the bundle's `metadata` is filled at build from the crate version and the git commit, so the binary always carries its matching skill. **Serving.** `website/public/skills/sakura/` is a committed byte-identical mirror (raw `SKILL.md` starts with the frontmatter, relative links work, nothing wraps it), beside `sakura-skill.zip` and `sakura-skill.sha256` and a content-only `manifest.json` with the compiler version, per-file SHA-256 hashes and ZIP SHA-256, without a Git commit (commit provenance is filled only at release/build time); the immutable URL of any revision is the repository's raw file at that commit (Git already is the versioned archive; the site never hosts per-commit copies); after the first release the ZIP is also a release asset. The agents landing page and `llms.txt` index link the skill, the install paths (`.claude/skills/sakura/`, `.agents/skills/sakura/`, `.cursor/skills/sakura/`) and the provenance. A Claude plugin and marketplace catalogue wrap the same files later (DX8, after the bundle is stable). **CI test** (`sakura_cli/tests/agent_docs.rs` over `compiler/tests/agent_docs/`): every complete code block in the body and references carries a fixture id and a mode (`check`, `test`, `run`, `wrong:<code>`, `fragment`, `hole-step`); unclassified fences fail; positive samples `check --json` to exit 0 with no error and no unexpected hole, `test`/`run` samples run offline with fakes and cassettes, wrong/fixed pairs assert the code and span then check the fixed program; frontmatter, relative links, JSON/TOML snippets, ZIP layout, checksums and mirror byte-equality are validated; no fixture may be skipped silently. Why: research/11 §3 (one source, several adapters; drift is the failure mode) and the Agent Skills specification's progressive disclosure; embedding in the binary is the only synchronisation that needs no network and no version negotiation. Rejected: a hand-written skill per client; a `scripts/` directory (the CLI is the script); a versioned archive on the site (Git); serving the skill through the `/docs/*.md` transformation (it wraps and rewrites); a `sakura doc --index` flag added only for the generator (the generator links the library). Performance: none at run time; the embedded bundle adds a few hundred kilobytes to the binary. Changed: §12.1 gains `sakura setup` through §17.143. Impact: Astra authors `SKILL.md` and the table, Opus writes `agent_docs` and the harness, Sol the mirror, ZIP, checksum and website links; plan DX1.

143. **DX2: `sakura new`/`init` write agent instructions; `sakura setup` installs the skill and client adapters, project-locally and opt-in (research/11 §4; round 10).** Decision: (1) `sakura new <kind> <name>` and `sakura init` also write, when absent, `AGENTS.md` (the research/11 §4 template prose, about 300 tokens: check the compiler version and read `sakura prompt`; use the `sakura` skill when installed; look up APIs with `sakura doc` or `symbol_query`; run `check --json`, `fmt --check`, `test --json`; finish holes before the final build; freeze specs for repairs; `explain CODE`; never widen contracts or authority to pass; verify `compiler_info.workspace_root` before trusting the MCP server) and `CLAUDE.md` containing the import line `@AGENTS.md`; existing files are never touched (`init` keeps its current rule: an existing `sakura.toml` is a usage error). (2) A new command, `sakura setup [--client claude|codex|cursor|all] [--apply]`, works in an existing project (it reads `sakura.toml`), prints what it would write and writes only with `--apply`: the embedded skill into `.claude/skills/sakura/`, `.agents/skills/sakura/` or `.cursor/skills/sakura/` per client; the MCP entry into `.mcp.json` (Claude), `.codex/config.toml` (Codex) or `.cursor/mcp.json` (Cursor), merging only the Sakura-owned `sakura` entry into an existing file and printing a patch instead when the file cannot be parsed; `.cursor/rules/sakura.mdc` for Cursor; `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` as in (1). It never edits `~/.claude.json`, `~/.codex/config.toml`, `~/.cursor/mcp.json` or any file outside the project, never pre-approves a server, never starts one. (3) **The workspace-root convention**: the generated Claude and Codex entries run `sakura mcp serve` with no `--root`; the server resolves its root as the nearest ancestor of its working directory that holds `sakura.toml` and reports it in `compiler_info.workspace_root`; the Cursor entry passes `--root ${workspaceFolder}` because Cursor documents that variable; no snippet ever contains a developer's absolute path. A client started outside the project gets a usage error from the server naming the directory it searched, not a silently wrong root. (4) `sakura new` still does not run `git init` (§17.63). Acceptance: scaffold tests cover the four templates with the two new files, existing-file preservation, `setup` on a fresh scaffold, `setup` twice (idempotent), each client's file merged into an existing one, a malformed existing config (patch printed, nothing written), a path with spaces, and `mcp serve` started from a subdirectory and from outside the project; a documented smoke run per client (research/11 §4's list) recorded in the plan with versions, by hand, before the agents landing page advertises the command. Why: research/11 §4 (small canonical instructions, exact client formats, no global side effects) and the `init` constraint (it is not an updater, so a separate command is the accepted behaviour). Rejected: `init --agents` (`init` refuses an existing manifest); writing global client configuration; a cross-client root variable; a launcher script. Performance: none. Changed: §12.1 (`sakura setup`, `new`/`init` outputs). Impact: Opus (`sakura_cli/src/new.rs`, a `setup.rs`, `sakura_mcp` root resolution), Sol (adapter templates under `skills/sakura/assets/`, the website quickstart), Astra (the template prose); plan DX2, after DX1's embedded bundle.

144. **DX3: an error-code reference page per registered code, generated from the registry, with the `tests/ui` fixture as the wrong program (research/11 §6; round 10).** Decision: `agent_docs` renders `website/content/docs/errors/<code>.mdx` for every entry of `codes::REGISTRY` (73 today, retired codes included and marked retired): title, phase, the registry's explanation, the `tests/ui/<code>_*.sakura` fixture as the wrong program with its expected JSON Lines diagnostic, and the fixed program from a sibling `tests/ui/<code>_<slug>.fixed.sakura` when one exists, which the ui harness checks to exit 0 (Sol adds fixed companions code by code, most frequent first; a page without one says so visibly rather than failing the build). An index page lists codes by phase. The pages are committed MDX, so the existing Fumadocs search, the `/docs/*.md` export and `llms-full.txt` cover them with no new route, and a website build on the shallow Vercel clone needs no `cargo`. `explanation_uri` stays `sakura://explain/<code>` in the JSON record (§12.2 is a versioned contract); the website, the skill and the editor map it to `/docs/errors/<code>`, and `sakura explain` prints the URL after its text. Acceptance: the generator's `--check` fails on a code without a page or a page without a code; the ui harness runs every fixed companion; link check over the index. Why: §12.3 promised "every code has an `explain` page with a minimal pair"; generating it from the registry is the only way the promise stays true. Rejected: a JSON export plus a dynamic route (a second pipeline for the website to maintain); changing the `sakura://` scheme to HTTP (a protocol change for a presentation need). Performance: none. Changed: §12.3 (the page is generated; the URL mapping). Impact: Opus (`agent_docs` registry export), Sol (fixed companions, index page, website wiring), Astra (repair guidance in explanations that are fragments today); plan DX3, with DX1's generator.

145. **DX4: a cookbook of task recipes, each an example the harness runs (research/11 §6; round 10).** Decision: recipes live under `compiler/examples/<recipe>/` in the existing fixture shape (manifest, `src/main.sakura`, tests, cassettes where needed) and are run by the examples harness like the twelve examples; `agent_docs` renders `website/content/docs/cookbook/<recipe>.mdx` and `skills/sakura/references/recipes.md` from them (source, the imports it needs, the `[permissions]` it declares, the failure branches it handles, how it is tested, and the compiler revision). First set, all offline with fakes or committed cassettes: parse and validate JSON into a refined record; a CLI over files; HTTP with retry and a cassette; a SQLite query with `sql"…"`; a router endpoint tested by calling the router; a scripted LLM tool; a budgeted agent loop; an indexed update loop with `List.set` (the §17.126 idiom, which the nbody port got wrong, §17.150). Recipes are distinct from the evaluation tasks of §17.146 (teaching a recipe must not reveal a held-out solution). Acceptance: every recipe passes its harness mode in CI; a recipe that uses an unfinished API is not published; the rendered pages regenerate clean. Why: agents copy; a copied recipe that runs is worth more than a paragraph. Rejected: prose-only recipes; recipes on the website only (they would rot). Performance: none. Changed: none. Impact: Astra writes recipes, Sol extracts and renders, Opus only where a recipe needs a missing tested example; plan DX4, after DX1.

146. **DX5: the agent evaluation pilot: offline oracles in CI, paid episodes only under an owner-approved budget (research/11 §5; round 10).** Decision: `evals/agent-dx/` at the repository root (recorded in ADR 0002), separate from `tools/sakura_eval` (a Core evaluator, not an agent benchmark): `tasks/<id>/{prompt.md, starter/, oracle/, task.json}`, `harness/`, `reports/`. `task.json` is an internal manifest documented in `evals/agent-dx/README.md` before implementation (id and revision, category, compiler revision and backend, starter hash, editable files, frozen baseline commit, oracle command, expected initial diagnostics, episode budgets, comparison eligibility); oracles are never on the agent's filesystem, and each starter commits a baseline so `--frozen-specs all --since <baseline>` has a real reference. **Tasks:** the twelve of research/11 §5 (two per category: syntax and data, diagnose and repair, holes and retrieval, small application change, deterministic effects, preserve assurance), with gold solutions validated and deliberately hollow solutions (removed tests, `.trust` shortcuts, widened permissions, leftover holes) proven rejected before any model runs. **Conditions:** baseline (no skill), guide (`sakura prompt`), skill (forced load and normal discovery, recorded separately), bootstrap (skill plus `AGENTS.md`), MCP (the best prior condition with the compiler MCP instead of the CLI, same information budget); ablations of cheat sheet, examples and repair table only on failing categories. **Metrics**, per fresh episode: pass@1 and budgeted episode success separately (repair turns never count as `k`), iterations to green (success fraction beside conditional median and p90; timeouts censored), diagnostic-fix success with specs and authority preserved, cost per accepted task (total spend over accepted tasks, undefined at zero), tokens including cached, activation and retrieval failures, unauthorised edits, termination reason. **Harness:** each episode in a disposable directory with network denied to the generated program, the toolchain pinned, the harness enforcing wall-clock and token limits itself (the compiler's budgets are soft), the provider broker outside the project holding the credentials; raw transcripts stored privately and redacted before any report is checked in. **Budget and approval:** the runner refuses to start without `--budget-usd <n>` and `--approved-by <name>`, estimates spend from the pinned prices before scheduling, stops when the cap cannot cover another episode, never retries unboundedly or escalates models; the GitHub workflow `agent-eval.yml` is `workflow_dispatch` only, runs in a protected environment whose required reviewer is the owner, takes the budget and the model identifiers as inputs, and never runs on pull requests. Pilot cap: 12 tasks × 2 conditions × 2 models × 1 episode = 48 episodes, 8 repair cycles, 12,000 output and 60,000 input tokens and 10 minutes per episode, 2 workers; a 3-sample replication (144 episodes) only after the harness is validated on a canned transcript. **Models:** one pinned Opus-family and one pinned GPT-6-Sol-family coding model as roles; the exact provider identifiers, reasoning and sampling settings, harness version and date are recorded per run and chosen at run time, never assumed here. **In CI on every PR, no secrets:** task schemas, gold and hollow oracles, the canned-transcript replay (accounting, timeouts, reporting), MCP smoke. **On demand, owner-approved:** model runs, the six-task TS/Python comparison (matched docs and tool budgets, pinned runtimes, Sakura-specific hole/effect/frozen-spec tasks scored separately), client UI smoke, held-out tasks. Twelve tasks are a diagnostic pilot, not a leaderboard; the report states per-task outcomes with raw counts and intervals, never a single score. Why: "agent-first" must be falsifiable (more independently correct changes per bounded cost, fewer repairs, no unauthorised spec changes), the Vercel and SWE-Skills-Bench results show activation and version mismatch dominate, and real models cost the owner money, so the harness makes approval a precondition rather than a convention. Rejected: a scheduled paid suite (a separate owner decision); counting repair turns as samples; a no-docs condition as the only baseline; inferring language superiority from a trained-in comparison. Performance: none. Changed: none. Impact: Opus (runner, isolation, accounting), Astra (task design, experiment protocol, report), Sol (fixtures, workflow, environment); plan DX5, offline parts after DX1 and DX2, paid runs when the owner approves.

147. **DX6: the first release is prepared end to end and waits for the owner's authorisation at every publishing step (research/11 §6; round 10).** Decision: (1) **Artifacts.** A `release.yml` triggered by a `v*` tag or `workflow_dispatch` in dry-run mode builds `sakura` for the owner-approved matrix (proposed: macOS arm64 and x86_64, Linux x86_64 and arm64 with musl, Windows x86_64), with `--locked`, the embedded std, `docs/llms.txt` and the skill from the same commit, SHA-256 sums, licence notices, and the skill ZIP; dry runs upload workflow artifacts only and create no release. A release note states the JavaScript host prerequisite (Node, the pinned major) for `run`/`test` on the JS target, and that `check`/`build`/`doc`/`mcp` need no host. (2) **Installer.** Astra evaluates `cargo-dist` first (it generates the workflow, archives, checksums, shell and PowerShell installers, a Homebrew tap formula and an npm wrapper from one configuration; uv is the precedent) against a hand-written `tools/install/` script; whichever is chosen must support a pinned version, checksum verification, platform detection, a non-interactive prefix, a plain download-and-extract path (never only `curl | sh`) and a clean failure on an unsupported target. (3) **Channels**, each prepared as a reviewed file and published only by the owner: a Homebrew tap formula in an owner-named tap with a smoke test (never assumed into core), an npm wrapper package with `bin`, `os`/`cpu` platform packages and a verified offline path (name reserved by the owner), the Claude plugin and marketplace catalogue (DX8), the VS Code extension (DX7). (4) **Versioning.** The first tag is `v0.1.0-alpha.1`; `STABILITY.md` says what an alpha promises (nothing beyond the versioned diagnostic schema and the fixture formats); the skill, the docs and the binary carry the same version. (5) **Acceptance** on a fresh host per platform: download → `sakura --version` → `new cli demo` → `check` → `test` → `build` → `mcp serve` smoke → `setup --apply` writes the skill; no published artifact is ever mutated. Why: research/11 §1 and §6: the first install is where adoption is lost, and a source build with a pinned toolchain is not an install story; publishing is the owner's act, so the work ends at a reviewed artifact. Rejected: publishing from CI on merge; a mutable "latest" release; a Homebrew core submission. Performance: none. Changed: none (`STABILITY.md` is the owner's to amend at the tag). Impact: Opus (workflow, installer logic), Sol (matrix, formula, wrapper, notices), Astra (evaluation and release docs), owner (matrix, namespaces, credentials, every publish); plan DX6, after DX2; the owner's questions are listed in the plan. **Amended (round 11, §17.157):** the matrix is the owner's GNU A→B→C (no musl); the tooling is the hand-written workflow of ADR 0003; crates.io is deferred for the alpha.

148. **DX7 and DX8: a VS Code client over the existing LSP now; tree-sitter, plugin packaging, the playground and Linguist behind their prerequisites (research/11 §6; round 10).** Decision: (1) `editors/vscode/`: a thin client that starts `sakura lsp` from the configured or `PATH` executable, registers `.sakura`, surfaces diagnostics with their `/docs/errors/<code>` link, hole context on demand, formatting and quick fixes with their impact class shown; the website's highlighting grammar is converted to its TextMate grammar; tests cover stdio start, root selection, UTF-16 positions on edits, stale fixes after an edit and a missing executable; installable from source (`vsce package` locally); marketplace publication is the owner's. (2) `editors/tree-sitter-sakura/` after (1): a grammar with a corpus derived from the compiler's lexer and parser cases (holes, tagged strings, comments, the §2.4 newline rule), tested against the CST on `tests/`; useful for editors and tooling, not a second parser of record. (3) The Claude plugin and marketplace (`plugins/sakura/`, `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`) wrap the DX1 bundle after it has been stable for one release; validated with `claude plugin validate`; the plugin version is independent of the compiler version. (4) The browser playground is **post-WASM and post-host**: it needs the checker compiled to WASM or a sandboxed checking service, a browser host with only pure effects and `Console.captured`, bounded worker execution with cancellation and no Node file or network bindings; the program-side WASM backend of M4 is necessary and not sufficient (research/11 §1). It starts after W2 parity and M4-OPT (`m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.7 items 3 and 7) with pure examples, diagnostics and share links; nothing before. (5) GitHub Linguist is deferred until the usage threshold is met honestly; no manufactured repositories. Why: the LSP exists and is tested; the rest has prerequisites research/11 §6 lists, and an early playground that cannot run effects would teach the wrong language. Rejected: a playground over the Node host; a tree-sitter grammar as the formatter's or LSP's parser. Performance: none. Changed: none. Impact: Opus (client, grammar), Sol (packaging), Astra (setup docs); plan DX7 after DX6's executable story, DX8 as stated.

149. **Ownership facts in Core: an owned `List`, `Map` or `Set` is updated in place on every backend, with the persistent operation as the fallback (research/10-evidence/perf-gaps-r2 rank 1; WS-P6 item 7 `map_set`; amends §17.134; round 10).** Question: `sieve` copies 1,592,937 path arrays (40.8 million slots) for 398,286 writes, and in-place writes on the unchanged trie are 5.82×/11.61× faster; `map_set` on Bun is ≈5.0 against a 3.0 ceiling because every HAMT insert copies 32-wide nodes where the control mutates a JS `Map`; §17.134 deferred any JS special case until the native reuse path existed. Decision: **one policy for `List`, `Map` and `Set`, decided now, in Core.** (1) A new deterministic pass `sakura_core::opt::owned` computes, per def, an *owned* fact for the consuming argument of each owned-capable builtin (`list_set`, `list_push`, `map_set`, `map_remove`, `set_add`, `set_remove`; the table in `sakura_core::builtins` gains two columns: `fresh`, a result that shares no node with any input, true for literals with elements, `list_range`, builder results and the string-splitting constructors, false for the empty singleton, `list_push`, `list_slice` and anything the runtime does not guarantee; and `consumes`, the argument position an owned-capable builtin takes over). A local is owned at that use when, in ANF order, it was bound by a `fresh` builtin, by an owned-capable builtin whose consumed argument was owned, or by a join parameter every incoming jump passes an owned value to (a fixpoint, widening to not-owned), and every other use of it before the consuming use is a *borrow* (a read builtin that retains nothing: `len`, `get`, `at`, `contains`, the §17.126 contract's `len`), with no capture by a closure, no storage into an allocation, no `Perform` argument, no `Call` of a non-builtin except a def whose parameter summary is *consuming* (a def consumes a parameter when the parameter's only non-borrow use is the consuming position of an owned-capable operation whose result is the def's result; computed bottom-up over the call graph without fixpoint for recursion, so `std/list.set`, which borrows `xs` in its contract and then consumes it, passes ownership through), and no further use after the consuming one. Anything else is not owned. (2) The fact is a per-argument annotation on `Call`/`Prim` computed at emission like `range` (§17.114), never part of the committed snapshot. The JS backend emits the owned entry (`list_set_owned`, `map_set_owned`, …: write the node in place, reuse the wrapper, invalidate the leaf cache and the entries cache, keep the insertion log consistent); the LIR calls the owning entry without a `Dup`, and the native runtime's `list_set`/`map_set` additionally test the reference count of every path node and copy the shared ones (the §17.126 dynamic path, which also catches what the static proof misses once RC exists). The persistent operation remains the fallback for every non-owned use, so value semantics (§8.1) are unchanged and no program can observe the mechanism. (3) `tools/sakura_eval` gains `Limits::audit_owned`: it tags every value an owned operation consumed and stops with `Internal` if the tagged value is read again, so the differential and corpus runs are a soundness oracle for the pass, as `audit_ranges` is for §17.114. (4) `sakura build --dump core` shows the annotation; `--dump direct`'s sibling `--dump owned` lists each consuming site as `owned` or `shared(<reason>)` with a fixed reason set (`origin`, `borrowed-after`, `captured`, `stored`, `escapes-call <name>`, `join`), so an agent can see why its loop copies. Invariants, each a fixture in `tests/semantics` with trace and `--no-direct` parity and a WASM run: a retained old version after `set`/`push`/`Map.set` is intact (including shared leaf and tail paths and a shared HAMT subtree); a list captured by a closure before the update; a list stored in a record or a `Map` value; the same owned loop run twice; the empty singleton (`push` on it allocates; `set` cannot reach it); an update inside `with`, `try` and after a `Perform` (the borrow analysis is lexical and per def, so handlers change nothing); a generic def updating a `List<A>`; `W8001` stays M5's. Expected effect: `sieve`'s path copies go (the remaining 5.2×/6.65× is representation, §17.152); `map_set`'s node copies go; `nbody` benefits after §17.150. Why: the proof the native path needs at M5 is the same proof the JS backend needs today; computing it once in Core (as §17.118 does for direct-ness) makes it a parity test between backends, and a static linear chain from a fresh origin is sound without reference counts because no other reference to any node can exist. Rejected: a mutable `Array`, a `Builder` value or an ownership annotation (§17.117, §17.126); inferring uniqueness from a `var` or a single syntactic use (a wrapper can be unique while its nodes are shared); the analysis in the LIR only (the JS backend does not pass through it); copy-on-write flat arrays as the representation now (§17.152 decides that question with numbers); waiting for M5 (§17.134's reason, the absence of a measure, no longer holds). Performance: `sieve` and `map_set` as above; a non-owned program pays one annotation at emission and nothing at run time. Changed: §8.1 (the mechanism named), §13.2 (the annotation), §13.4 (the owned entries), §17.134 (amended), §17.116 item (2) corrected to the insertion log (WS-P6 deviation 2; §13.4's `String.len` row already says O(n)). Impact: `sakura_core` (`opt/owned.rs`, builtins columns), `sakura_codegen_js` (owned entries), `runtime/js` (`*_owned`, cache invalidation, `ABI.md` §7), `sakura_lir` and `runtime/rt` (owning entries, per-node rc test in `list.rs`/`map.rs`, `builtins.sigs`), `tools/sakura_eval` (`audit_owned`); plan `performance-r1.md` §9 WS-P7 (Opus), CODEOWNERS review as a semantics-adjacent change.

150. **Benchmark sources are fixed under the WS-A definition rule when they use a pre-`List.set` idiom or an unchecked control: `nbody` and `countdown` (perf-gaps-r2 rank 2; WS-P4 deviation 8; round 10).** Decision: (1) `bench/programs/nbody/sakura/main.sakura`'s local `set` (`concat(push(slice(bs, 0, i), b), slice(bs, i + 1, len(bs)))`, written before §17.126 added `List.set`) becomes `List.set(bs, i, b)`; the change is a **benchmark-definition boundary** recorded exactly as `5b10553` was for `sieve` (`research/10-evidence/ws-a/nbody-definition-change.json` with both blob hashes; pre-change `nbody` ratios excluded from every comparison and noise summary; the raw runs retained; `bench/deltas.md` must not list it as an optimisation). It is allowed because §17.109's matched lane compares the language's canonical idiom against the control's, and a port that reconstructs a vector through three O(n) operations measures a legacy idiom, not the language. (2) `bench/programs/countdown/js/main.mjs` accumulates with raw `acc += i` while the Sakura program checks every `+` and `-`; §17.109 requires the control to detect overflow at the same points, so the control gains the explicit check (`Number.isSafeInteger` after each operation, as the `fib` note in `bench/README.md` already flags), with the same boundary record; Sol audits the other fourteen controls for the same omission in one pass and records each. (3) No compiler or std change is made for the `slice`/`concat` idiom: `List.slice` is O(n) by contract and §17.103 tolerates a cost paid at the operation that asks for it; the skill's cheat sheet and the DX4 recipe teach `List.set` (§17.142, §17.145), which is where an agent learns the idiom. Why: a benchmark that measures the wrong program is noise with a decimal point; the precedent and its rules exist. Rejected: rewriting `slice`-then-`concat` chains in `opt` (invalid without bounds and evaluation-order proofs); claiming the nbody delta as a compiler win. Performance: `nbody` 2.79×/3.30× on the unchanged compiler, a definition change; `countdown`'s ratio changes by the control's added check. Changed: none. Impact: Sol (`bench/programs/nbody`, `countdown/js`, the audit, the boundary records), Astra (ledger notes); plan `performance-r1.md` §9.

151. **`List.get` immediately consumed by `Option.expect` fuses into one checked read that uses the leaf cache (perf-gaps-r2 rank 3; round 10).** Question: `spectral_norm`'s 6,401,600 reads each build `Some(v)` and a payload array, then unwrap it; fusing the pair is 2.84×/6.30× (2.28×/4.56× without the cache). Decision: a peephole in `sakura_core::opt` on ANF after inlining: `let o = list_get(xs, i); let r = option_expect(o, msg)` with `o` used exactly once becomes `let r = list_get_expect(xs, i, msg)`, a new builtin in both runtimes whose failure is `D030` with the same message and the same blame site, whose argument evaluation order is unchanged (ANF already fixed it), and whose success path reads through the cached traversal (`list_at`'s leaf cache, WS-P6 deviation 1). The same rule covers `map_get` + `option_expect`. An `Option` that escapes, is matched, or is used twice is never fused. `tools/sakura_eval` implements the builtin as the composition, so the differential run is the oracle; fixtures: in range, out of range (`D030`, message, span and exit 70 identical under `--no-direct` and on WASM), the Option used twice (not fused; `--dump core` shows it), a `Map` key miss. Why: the allocation is the cost, the cache is the second cost, and the fusion is local, deterministic and invisible. Rejected: changing `List.get`'s type; a source-level `xs[i]` that defects (it is `Option.expect` by sugar today, and the fusion makes it cost what an indexing operator would). Performance: `spectral_norm` to roughly 3.4× Node / 2.1× Bun of hand JS on the perf-gaps-r2 numbers; the remaining gap is §17.152's. Changed: §13.2 (the builtin is an `opt` product, not a Core form). Impact: `sakura_core` (`opt` peephole, builtins table), `runtime/js`, `runtime/rt` + `sakura_lir` `builtins.sigs`, `tools/sakura_eval`; plan `performance-r1.md` §9 WS-P8 (Opus), small.

152. **`sieve`, `nbody` and `spectral_norm` are representation-bound; their §17.109 ceilings are carried to the representation decision, and the JS close-out is judged on the other twelve programs plus the suite median (perf-gaps-r2; round 10).** Question: after §17.149–§17.151 land, perf-gaps-r2's ablations put `sieve` at about 5.2×/6.65× (in-place trie writes; 2.70×/1.51× only with a flat private array), `nbody` at about 9×/9× (indexed set plus owned lists; 5.3×/6.5× only with record reuse too), and `spectral_norm` at about 3.4×/2.1× (1.22×/1.07× only with hoisted flat reads and interprocedural range and congruence proofs), against ceilings of 1.5, 1.3 and 1.3. Decision: (1) the three ceilings are neither waived nor met: they are **carried** as the acceptance of a representation round that the M5 preparation (`m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.7 item 11) must now answer with numbers: whether `List` keeps the 32-way trie or becomes a flat, value-semantic sequence updated in place when owned (Lean's `Array`, Koka's vector; copy on a shared update, O(n)), what the persistent-workload cost of each is (`rbtree` retained versions, `pipeline`, the §17.116 builders), and whether records are reused under §17.149's facts (rank 4); nothing changes representation before that round, on any backend. (2) The performance round's JS acceptance (§14.3, plan §5) reads: the median of the fifteen ratios ≤ 1.5 and no program **other than** `sieve`, `nbody` and `spectral_norm` above its ceiling; those three are reported with their post-landing ratios and the named remaining cause, in `bench/deltas.md`. (3) `map_set` keeps its 3.0 ceiling on Node; on Bun the ceiling is **5.0, provisional**, because WS-P6's profile puts 37% of the Bun time in JSC string hashing that the control pays once per string object through the engine's cache and Sakura pays per operation; §17.149 removes the node copies, and the quiet-window remeasure (§17.153) of the three key kinds separately decides whether the provisional number stands or returns to 3.0. (4) `pipeline`'s 2.0 is unchanged: WS-P6's 2.98 on Bun predates WS-P4's inline fast path for the `for` counter and is remeasured before anything is concluded. Why: a ceiling that only a representation change can meet is a design question for the representation, not a defect of the optimisation round, and §17.109 made each ceiling "a gate for claiming competitiveness", which these three programs do not claim. Rejected: raising the three ceilings to the ablation numbers (it would bless a representation by default); copy-on-write arrays now (§13.4's note: measured, and the persistent cost side is not); removing the programs from the suite. Performance: none. Changed: §14.3 (performance round acceptance), §17.109 (pointer). Impact: Astra (the representation research, `research/12-m5-native.md`, with these numbers as its brief), WS-B (per-key-kind reporting for `map_set`); plan `performance-r1.md` §5 and §9.

153. **Measurement policy under agent load: ceilings and baselines are judged only in a quiet window on the reference machine; CI and loaded-host runs are directional (WS-P4 deviation 8, WS-P6 item 6; round 10).** Question: with ten agents on the shared machine (one-minute load 10–11), `bench compare` of a build against itself blocks, and every workstream's before/after is taken under a different load. Decision: (1) **Authoritative numbers** come from a *quiet window*: the owner pauses the agents, Astra runs the full suite with `--stable` under `record_load.py`, and the run is quiet when every 15-second snapshot's one-minute load is at most a quarter of the logical CPU count (3.0 on the 12-CPU reference machine) and no other build, benchmark or agent process appears in the busiest-executable list; the harness records the verdict in the result's `meta.host.quiet` and `bench bless` refuses a run that is not quiet. One quiet window closes a batch of landings (the close-out in `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.7 item 6, then one per round), not one per workstream. (2) **CI** (`bench.yml`, §17.110) keeps its ratio-to-ratio policy on pull requests, which cancels most runner variance; its artefacts are trend evidence, never a bless; a weekly scheduled `noise` run (five repeats, the existing manual job on a schedule) characterises the runners, which is also the §17.136 Bun study's CI half. (3) **Loaded-host runs** by implementers are *advisory*: `bench compare` records the recorded load in its verdict and prints `advisory (host load L)` instead of `blocked` or `ok` when the base or head run exceeded the quiet threshold; an implementer may conclude the *direction* of a change from an advisory run when the improvement exceeds the noise band of the ledger's unchanged-build study, and may conclude nothing about a ceiling. (4) **Acceptance meanwhile**: a performance workstream *lands* on deterministic evidence (semantics, trace and `--no-direct` parity, the `sakura_eval` audits, no CI block, an advisory before/after posted in REQUESTS and `bench/deltas.md`) and is *accepted against its ceiling* only at the next quiet window; the plan tracks the two states separately, and no ceiling is declared met from an advisory run. Why: a gate that fires on unchanged code is ignored (§17.136), the agents' own load is the dominant noise source on this machine, and a quiet window costs the owner minutes while a dedicated runner costs money the round does not have. Rejected: a dedicated benchmarking host or service (reconsidered when history across machines matters, §17.110); stopping other agents' processes from a harness (never); a wider block threshold (hides regressions); per-workstream quiet windows (the owner's time). Performance: none. Changed: §17.110 (pointer), plan `performance-r1.md` §3.4 and §5. Impact: WS-B (`meta.host.quiet`, `bless` refusal, advisory verdict with the load; Opus), Sol (`bench.yml` weekly schedule), Astra (the quiet-window protocol in `bench/README.md`, the ledger), owner (announces the window).

154. **`countdown`: `opt::ranges` seeds parameter intervals from `requires`; no induction; mutual tail recursion is measured first (WS-P4 deviation 3 and 8; round 10).** Question: `countdown` is 1.57 Node / 2.75 Bun against 1.3 because the per-def pass cannot prove `n - 1` and `acc + n`; should it gain loop or recursion induction for guarded counters. Decision: (1) No induction: in `sum_tail(n, acc)` as written, `n - 1` underflows for a negative `n` and `acc + n` overflows for `n` above about 2^26, so the checks are the semantics, not a weakness of the pass; a sound proof needs a fact the caller supplies, and the language already has the place for it. (2) `opt::ranges` therefore seeds a parameter's interval from the def's `requires` clauses (and from a refined parameter's `where`, both evaluated before the body, §17.115) when the clause is a conjunction of comparisons of the parameter against literals or other seeded parameters; `fn sum_tail(n: Int, acc: Int) -> Int requires n >= 0` then proves `n - 1` under the `n == 0` guard's else branch (interval `[1, ∞)`), while `acc + n` stays checked unless `acc` is bounded too. The seeding is intraprocedural, syntactic and sound because the contract is checked at entry (`D020`); `--dump core` shows the seeded interval. (3) The control's missing overflow check is fixed by §17.150, and `is_even`/`is_odd` (a two-def component that runs direct for `LIMIT` frames and hands the rest to the CPS twin, §17.112) is the suspected remainder; the quiet-window remeasure records `countdown`'s two halves separately (two sizes of one program, or `--dump direct` plus a split timing by Astra). If the mutual half dominates, a follow-up turns tail calls between members of a direct component into jumps into one merged loop with a dispatch parameter (the classic transform; deterministic, bounded code size, mirrored in the LIR under §17.118), as WS-P9 after WS-P7; if not, nothing. The 1.3 ceiling stands. Why: the pass must not prove what is false, and the fastest honest path is the contract an agent can write in one line. Rejected: loop induction without a bound (unsound for negative inputs); widening `Int` or dropping checks (§17.117); a `@bounded` annotation. Performance: `countdown` loses one check per iteration when its author states the precondition; programs without contracts are unchanged. Changed: §17.114 (pointer). Impact: `sakura_core/src/opt/ranges.rs` (WS-P4b, Opus, small, with `audit_ranges` fixtures for a seeded `requires` that is violated at run time and for a parameter without a clause); plan `performance-r1.md` §9.

155. **W1/R4 readings endorsed; runtime-internal drives spend no trace task number; `V8004` is the code; `Secret` transport across the host boundary is an owner gate item (W1 `ded31de`, REQUESTS 2026-10-18 W1 and W1 follow-up; round 11).** Question: (1) §17.80 numbers tasks in creation order, but JS's `satisfies` runs every refinement predicate of a JSON decode through `runTask(r.check, [v])`, which allocates a task, so a program that decodes a refined type and then spawns carries different task numbers on JS and on WASM (the host runs the predicate outside the guest), and `tests/parity.rs` compares task numbers only up to renaming in order of first appearance. (2) Plan §3.1 named the refusal of `@external(js, …)` on `--target wasm` `V8002`, which §17.131 gave to `reserved_package_name`; W1 registered `V8004 target_unsupported_external` "as a placeholder". (3) A `Secret` crosses `sakura-vt1` as tag 14 and carries its inner value only to an import of kind `secret` (today `js_handler`, which builds `Llm.anthropic` on the host); every other import receives `14, Unit`; W1 asks for security review. (4) `String.parse_int` on WASM returns `Some` for every i64. Decision: (1) **Trace task numbers count the tasks the program creates.** A number is spent for the main task, a test wrapper, the children of `Task.spawn`/`scope`/`all`/`race`, `expect_defect`'s child and each property case. A drive the runtime starts for its own purposes whose body cannot perform (a refinement predicate in `satisfies`, a `Schema` check, any later pure helper) runs under the current task's identity (the `$rt.T` register of §17.113; an unnumbered scratch task when no task is running, which is the WASM host's codecs with tracing off) and spends no number. JS changes; WASM already behaves so. §17.80's sentence reads "tasks are numbered in creation order of the tasks the program creates". Why: the trace exists so that two backends can be compared record for record (§17.80), and a task that by construction never writes a record must not shift the numbers of the tasks that do; the only honest fix is on the side that invented the extra task. Rejected: keeping the renaming in `parity.rs` (a comparison up to renaming cannot see a real task-order divergence, which is what the trace is for); numbering internal drives on WASM too (the guest would spend ids for host work, and under ABI §8.1 the host decodes refined records regardless). Acceptance: `satisfies` and every other internal `runTask` whose body cannot perform drive without `newTask()`; a `D010` inside the predicate is still attributed to the decoding task; `parity.rs` compares task numbers exactly and its renaming is deleted; a `tests/semantics` fixture decodes a refined record, then spawns and performs in the child, with the trace identical on both backends; `runtime.test.js` pins that a decode allocates no task. (2) **`V8004 target_unsupported_external` is the code**, not a placeholder: 800x is the package/build group (`V8001` entry point, `V8002` manifest name, `V8003` ABI version), 802x is frozen specs, and a target refusal belongs with the build codes. The placeholder sentence leaves the registry explanation, plan §3.1 says `V8004`, the DX3 page regenerates. Why: the code is registered, constructed, tested and on a generated page; renumbering would churn four surfaces for no reader. (3) **`Secret` transport across the WASM host boundary is a gate item for the owner's security review** (gate 10's checklist in `docs/reviews/m3-security-review.md`, under its existing `Secret<T>` row): the designer records the reading (tag 14; the inner value only to a kind-`secret` import, `js_handler` today, as ABI §8.1 lists; redacted to `14, Unit` otherwise, so cassette lookups see that a header is secret and never its value; `runtime/rt/tests/vt.rs` pins both directions) and its risk (a kind-`secret` import is the one place a secret leaves the guest in clear, so each such import is a reviewed addition), and does **not** sign it off. Until the owner's signature: no new kind-`secret` import, no widening of what `js_handler` receives, and the host zeroes the slice after the call as ABI §8.1 says. (4) **§17.44 is read per backend**, as §17.80 reads `D003`/`D001`: on WASM `String.parse_int` and `Float.to_int_checked` answer `Some` for every i64, the differential keeps skipping such programs on WASM, and a fixture that depends on the range carries `@js`/`@wasm` expectations. The remaining W1/R4 readings (plan §7 "W1 and R4" items 1–16: the linker lowering libcore's sign extension, saturating truncation and `call_indirect` encoding to the §1 profile; the L3/L12 calling convention for compiled code; catch entries carrying their exits; the static block; the `start`/`outcome`/`tests`/`stats` payloads; the extra `sakura_host` imports; tagged `sakura-vt1` with host-side codecs; fibers and virtual time; the JS-built handler protocol with `Llm.stream` as `D090`) are **endorsed as built**; the R3 hand-built tests' older convention is retired when W2 next touches them. Performance: one task allocation fewer per decoded refined value on JS. Changed: §17.80 (pointer), §12.3 (`V8004`). Impact: `runtime/js/sakura_runtime.js` (`satisfies`; Opus, with W2, small), `sakura_cli/tests/parity.rs`, one `tests/semantics` fixture, `sakura_diagnostics` (one sentence), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.1; the owner (gate item).

156. **WS-P2 close-out: the cancellation acceptance reworded, the resumable loop as WS-P2b, the `handled_suspend` peak judged as young-generation sizing under a stated protocol, §17.138 narrowed to call consumers (WS-P2 `0d30acf`/`67155a0`/`ce9ebe1`, deviations 5, 8, 9, 10, 13; round 11).** Decision: (1) **Plan §5 item 3 is reworded**: "a cancelled task never runs a bound perform's clause, and every bound perform remains a cancellation point: the dispatch tests the current task's cancellation flag before the clause (pinned by the runtime unit test), and `handler_cancel_bound.sakura` shows cancellation, finalizer and trace records identical to the unbound form." The old wording assumed a cancellation can arrive while a task runs; in the JS runtime a task is cancelled only while suspended (`cancelTask` reaches a suspended task, which unwinds at its resume), so the first operation that *observes* a cancellation is the resumption, by construction, in every program. The guarantee the design needs (§8.6: every effect operation is a cancellation point; §17.113: bound dispatch does not lose it) is the test before the clause, which the fixture and the unit test pin. Why: an acceptance no correct implementation can meet measures nothing. (2) **WS-P2b, the resumable loop.** A loop (join point) in a CPS body whose only CPS steps are unbound performs or `cps` builtins is emitted as a `for (;;)` with a resumable entry: on the fast path (handler found, clause direct) the iteration continues inside the loop; on the slow path the loop's live state is captured once in a continuation that re-enters the loop at its head, so an iteration pays no join call and no closure unless it actually suspends or forwards to a CPS clause. Rules: per join point, deterministic, only when every other bind of the loop body is direct-emittable (§17.111's `Facts`), with blame spans, `Check` binds, the trace and the gauge accounting unchanged (an iteration that re-enters through the continuation counts one frame, otherwise none). Acceptance: `handled_fwd` and `handled_suspend` emit the loop form (an emitted-JS snapshot); `handler_cancel_in_loop`, `handler_cancel_bound`, `million_operations`, the nested-`with` case and every `tests/semantics` fixture unchanged under `--no-direct`, trace parity and on WASM; a new fixture suspends twice in one loop and resumes with the right state; advisory deltas on `handled_fwd` (9.02×/14.33× today) and `handled_suspend` (5.18×/4.07×), judged at the next quiet window against the `handled` band (2.0 at depth 1, 3.0 at depth 16; the forwarding and suspend cases are reported beside it, they have no ceiling of their own in §17.109); the `handled_suspend`/Node RSS cell remeasured and the per-iteration closures confirmed or ruled out as the allocation-rate cause. Owner Opus, `emit.rs` (reading `direct.rs`'s `Facts` only), **after WS-P5 lands** (WS-P5 edits `emit.rs` for blame); plan `performance-r1.md` §9. Rejected: polling `T.cancelled` per iteration without a perform (the perform is the cancellation point); doing it in Core (the loop form is an emission choice; Core's join points are the LIR's input and the LIR does its own under §17.118). (3) **`handled_suspend`/Node peak RSS (+37%) is judged as young-generation sizing, not a retained-memory regression, under a stated protocol.** §17.136 keeps the maximum gate and the confirmation group, and a confirmed Node crossing stays a block *unless* the implementer shows all three of: (a) the head's post-GC heap equals the base's (here 5.0 MB both, 60 runs); (b) `--trace-gc` attributes the whole rise to new-space growth (a semi-space doubling) with no additional mark-compact and no old-generation growth; (c) under a capped semi space (`--max-semi-space-size`, diagnostic only, never in the harness or a baseline) base and head peak equal (69–70 MB both). WS-P3's `binary_trees` would have failed (b) and (c): its dead temporaries were pretenured into the old generation, which is exactly what the protocol separates from nursery sizing. When the three hold, the cell is a **warning carrying the diagnostic**, recorded in `bench/deltas.md` with the three readings and the commits, and the baseline takes the new number at the next owner bless (the quiet window) like every other change; the harness is unchanged (no exemption list, no flag): the ruling is the designer's per cell, as a definition boundary is (§17.150). WS-P2's RSS block is therefore closed as a warning; WS-P3's `binary_trees` block closes when Astra confirms WS-P2's 157–168 MB at the quiet window (§17.138). Why: an allocation-rate change that makes V8 grow its nursery is the engine's heuristic answering a faster program, which §17.136 already recognised on Bun; the protocol keeps the gate's power on the case it caught. Rejected: a median gate; making Node advisory like Bun (the stable engine stays the gate); re-blessing before the quiet window; ignoring the block.
