(4) **§17.138 is amended**: the consumer must be a **call** (`Call`, or `Prim` of a function), never an allocation: with `new Tree(1, [r, bottom_up$d(…)])` V8 allocates the literal before evaluating the nested call, so the producer's result survives the whole subtree, and `binary_trees`/Node n=14 measured 257–290 MB that way against 157–168 MB with calls only (deviation 9). A producer that sets the blame site keeps its `$S.s` line in front of the consumer, and a consumer that sets the site is not inlined into. (5) The named maintainer review of WS-P2 (plan §5 item 6) is still owed and is listed for the owner with the gate item of §17.155 (3). Performance: as measured by WS-P2 (d1/d4/d16 at 0.84–0.86× Node, 0.95–1.46× Bun); `handled_fwd` and `handled_suspend` to WS-P2b. Changed: §17.113 (pointer), §17.138 (amended), plan `performance-r1.md` §5 item 3, §8 (pointers), §9 (WS-P2b). Impact: Opus (WS-P2b), Astra (the `deltas.md` row with the protocol's three readings; the quiet-window confirmation of `binary_trees`), owner (review).

157. **DX6 recorded: hand-written release tooling (ADR 0003); the owner's GNU matrix A→B→C supersedes §17.147's musl proposal; crates.io is deferred for the alpha (research/13 `d47ff83`, DX6 `fb4c4e9`..`474a8b1`, REQUESTS 2026-10-18 DX6 research and DX6; round 11).** Decision: (1) **ADR 0003** records the tooling decision: a hand-written `release.yml`, `tools/release/release.py` and `tools/install/install.sh` driven by one inventory, `packaging/targets.json`, over cargo-dist 0.33.0, for the reasons research/13 §1 established by source inspection (its shell installer succeeds without verification when `sha256sum` is absent and accepts a missing checksum; its PowerShell installer compares no checksum; its npm wrapper downloads at install time instead of carrying the binary; its stock publishing has no per-channel owner gate). Reconsidered when a cargo-dist release verifies unconditionally in both installers and its npm packaging carries binaries; status Proposed until the owner accepts it in review. (2) **§17.147 (1) is amended to the owner's matrix**: A `aarch64-apple-darwin` and `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`; B adds `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu`; C adds `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` and `x86_64-apple-darwin`; each stage after the previous one has shipped and been accepted on a fresh host. GNU, not musl: the archives are not static, the installer and the npm launcher refuse musl with a message naming the reason (as built), and Alpine is unsupported at the alpha; a musl stage, if ever, is a later owner decision with its own qualification. (3) **crates.io is deferred for `v0.1.0-alpha.1`**: the channels are the GitHub release archives with the installer, the Homebrew tap and npm. Why: `cargo publish` of `sakura_cli` is impossible as the workspace is structured (unpublished path dependencies; `include_str!` and build scripts read std, the runtimes, `docs/llms.txt` and `skills/` from outside the crate root), the two ways to make it possible (a generated self-contained source package `sakuralang` whose generator copies the embedded inputs into the package root, or publishing every internal crate with registry versions) are each an architecture decision with STABILITY and licensing consequences, and no consumer needs it today: agents and people get the binary from the installer, npm or the tap, and a `cargo install --path` user builds from the repository with the pinned toolchain. A placeholder or name-reservation crate and a crate whose build downloads the compiler are forbidden (research/13 §2 item 3). When: revisited for the first beta, or earlier if a `cargo install sakuralang` request exists, through a new ADR whose default to research is the generated source package (reviewed `cargo package --list`, provenance inside the package, `cargo publish --dry-run --locked` as the acceptance). Meanwhile `release.yml` keeps no crates job (as built), `docs/release.md` marks the crates.io channel "deferred (§17.157)" and drops the `release-crates` environment from the one-time setup, and the "reduced first-release channels" acceptance research/13 asks of the owner is the owner's acknowledgement of this entry in REQUESTS. (4) Recorded for the owner, not decided here: Blacksmith is an owner installation and cost check before any Linux CI switch, with release builds and every publishing job staying on GitHub-hosted runners (npm trusted publishing requires them); signing stays SHA-256 over every asset plus GitHub artifact attestations on the approved publication, with unsigned/not-notarized status stated plainly, until the owner chooses a key. Rejected: publishing internal crates (STABILITY keeps them unpublished; it multiplies names and release ordering); restructuring the workspace now for a channel without a consumer; a musl build labelled static. Performance: none. Changed: §17.147 (amended), ADR 0003, plan `agent-dx.md` §3 (answer), `docs/release.md` (Sol, two sentences). Impact: Sol (`docs/release.md`, CHANGELOG line if the channel list is user-visible there), owner (ADR acceptance, environments, the dispatch dry run on GitHub, name ownership).

158. **Order after round 11 (round 11).** In flight: WS-P7 (ownership, §17.149), WS-P5 (contracts, §17.115), M4-W2 (WASM parity), DX7 (VS Code). Next, in this order and with these gates: WS-P8 (fusion, §17.151) now, it is small and lives in `opt.rs`, and its peephole runs before WS-P2's emission; WS-P2b (§17.156) after WS-P5 lands, because both edit `emit.rs`; L4 (§17.118) after WS-P5, `direct.rs` is settled since WS-P2 and WS-P2b changes emission, not facts; M4-TEST (b) after W2's first green local run; the quiet-window close-out when WS-P7, WS-P8 and WS-P5 have landed (WS-P4b, WS-S10 and WS-B10 are on master), **without waiting for WS-P2b**: the forwarding and suspend cases carry no ceiling of their own and are reported with their cause, and the `handled_suspend` RSS cell is a warning under §17.156 (3); M4-OPT after W2 and L4; the M4 envelope after M4-OPT; DX8 as `agent-dx.md` states. Nothing else moves. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.7 item 12 and its dependency line, plan `performance-r1.md` §9. Impact: none beyond the routing.
159. **Gate 10 passed under the owner's delegation; `Secret` transport on WASM approved; `Secret.map` becomes audited; the test runner always passes its permissions (security review `docs/reviews/m3-security-review.md`, REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "gate 10"; round 12).** Question: gate 10 is the owner's signature on the M3 security packet (§17.128); on 2026-10-18 the owner delegated it in writing ("u do the security review i dont mind"), and §17.155 (3) had routed the WASM `Secret` transport to the same gate. Decision: (1) **Gate 10 is passed.** Parts A (escape rules), B (cassette format) and C (`Untrusted` in templates, with §17.130's `V2033`) are approved as the rules in force, reviewed against `6ccce80` and recorded as "reviewed and signed by Claude Fable 5.1 under the owner's written delegation of 2026-10-18", never as the owner's personal review; the owner can overturn any line. M3 is complete, the §17.128 freeze is lifted, and the review's findings, none in a frozen file, are plan WS12. The review was adversarial: four Medium findings outside the packet's parts and four on the WASM boundary are required work before a published release or before WASM parity is called complete. (2) **`Secret.map` takes a `Trust` reason.** A pure closure cannot perform, but it can defect with the clear value in its message (`Secret.map(token, fn(t) { Option.expect(None, t) })` prints the token in the `D030` record), so `map` was an unaudited egress; with a reason it is listed by `sakura audit` beside `expose`. §6.4's sentence "prevents accidental disclosure only" stays true and now has no exception for a deliberate program short of `@trust`. (3) **`sakura test` always sets `SAKURA_PERMISSIONS`**: without `--record`, `fs` grants empty, `net` and `env` empty, so a test that installs `Env.host()`, `Fs.host()` or `Http.host()` itself fails closed by the runtime's checks, not only by Node's permission model, which `node:sqlite` bypasses (a test wrote a database file into the package directory). `Test.fixtures()` keeps its own grants. (4) **WASM carries `Secret` headers**: `http_send` and `http_stream_open` become imports of kind `secret`. Under the stated trust model the host is the trusted base and owns linear memory, so the `14, Unit` redaction is hygiene against honest host paths (cassettes, logs, traces, `show`), the same guarantee the JS `Secret` class gives; making the two HTTP imports kind-`secret` is the egress the JS backend's `fetchArgs` already is, the cassette imports stay redacted, and `js_handler` already crosses the `Llm.anthropic` key the same way. The kind-`secret` list is `js_handler`, `http_send`, `http_stream_open`, and no import joins it without a line in the review record. ABI §8.1 is corrected (1.0.3 erratum): the host drops its references rather than "zeroes its copy" (a JS string cannot be zeroed), there is no LIR check (redaction is dynamic), and the guest zeroes its transport payload. (5) **`Sql.sqlite`'s lazy `Fs.root()` runs in the clause's task** on both backends (the W2 open question): JS's `sql_sqlite` performs it under the current task's identity as §17.155 (1) already requires of runtime-internal drives, and `tests/wasm.rs` compares that trace with task numbers. (6) The `serve` host gets an explicit request-body cap (`413`) and survives an invalid handler reply (`500`), a cassette exchange line is validated at `open`, the proof key's directory mode and owner are checked as the file's are, and the workflows outside `release.yml` pin action SHAs; the installer verifies the GitHub attestation when `gh` is present. Why: the delegation is a reason to review carefully, not a reason to wave anything through; the three frozen rules were found to be the right ones and enforced, and holding M3 open for findings in unfrozen files would freeze code the findings do not touch. Rejected: "changes requested" on the packet (no finding is in a reviewed rule, and WS9b would re-freeze code the fixes never touch); keeping the WASM refusal (it bought no security the trust model can state and broke `Http.bearer`/`Llm.anthropic` over `Http.host()` on WASM); redacting defect messages at run time instead of auditing `map` (a message is the program's text, and the audit site is the invariant §6.4 names); dropping `Secret.map` (std sinks and derived credentials need it). Performance: none at run time beyond one body-length check per served request. Changed: §6.4 (`Secret.map` signature), §10.2 (`Env`/`Fs`/`Http` host handlers under `sakura test`), §17.128 (closed), §17.155 (3) (decided), ABI §8.1/§8.4 erratum (M4-RT), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §2 WS12, §3.7, §4; gate report gate 10. Impact: Opus (WS12 items 1–5, 7, 8), Sol (item 6), the owner (review-record T5, T8); `website/content/docs/roadmap.mdx` and `tour/memory.mdx` (M3 complete; WASM `Secret` wording after item 7 lands).
160. **L4 endorsed: twins are the mechanism, M4-OPT keeps the bar; the restart fallback stands with a counter; the byte gauge stands; ABI 1.1.0 is the ABI; `countdown` is WS-P9 (REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "L4"; plan §7 L4 items 2–5, 7; round 12).** Decision: (1) **Split.** "Frame reuse in direct regions" (§17.118) is read as L4 built it: no frames in a direct region, each region compiled a second time as a native twin, twins calling twins. L4 delivered the mechanism; M4-OPT owns the §17.118 bar (≥ 2× over `--no-direct` on `fib`, `countdown`, `nbody`) and decides, by measurement, whether lambdas, clauses, bound performs and closure calls of function-typed parameters need twins to reach it. `fib` already meets the bar (0.10 s against 0.40 s); `nbody` (0.18 s against 0.29 s) and `countdown` (0.12 s against 0.13 s) do not, and each has a named cause: `countdown` is (5) below; `nbody`'s frames fell from 52,038 to 7,005 while `boxes` did not move, so M4-OPT profiles it first, and if the remaining cost is the boxed generic words of `List<Float>` elements that ABI §4 keeps until M5 (§17.93), the `nbody` bar is **carried to M5 with §17.152's representation decision, stated in `bench/deltas.md`**, never quietly missed. (2) **The restart fallback is endorsed.** A twin that runs out of gauge returns `ABORTED` through every twin above it and the frame-protocol caller makes the same call through frames; direct code performs nothing, so the second run is unobservable, and a defect in the first attempt is the one the frame path would hit first (the same code, the same site). Bound: the work is at most doubled once per entry from frame code and only for regions `func::may_abort` cannot clear, and the arguments are held only then. To keep that bound measurable, M4-OPT adds a `restarts` counter to `stats()` (ABI 1.2.0, MINOR, appended after `drops`) and the benchmark record carries it; a program whose `restarts` is not 0 or 1 per entry is a bug, not a cost. ABI §5's "the caller rewrites the call" is corrected to what is built in the same bump. (3) **The gauge counts estimated native stack bytes** (`64 + 8 × variables` per twin, budget 128 KiB) rather than 400 frames: the two limits are each backend-local and neither is observable in a program (the fallback is frames, not a defect), so there is no parity question; the 512 KiB Wasmtime default stack is the stated assumption and `deep_recursion_gauge` is the pin. A host with a smaller stack is a host configuration, not a language case. (4) **ABI 1.1.0 is endorsed**: the four counters (`frames`, `boxes`, `dups`, `drops`), the direct-style paragraph of §5, `sakura_abi_version` `0x010100`. §10's `sakura run --stats` does not exist and is deleted in the 1.2.0 wording; `SAKURA_WASM_STATS=<path>` is the interface, and the JS lane reports no frames, boxes or counts. (5) **`countdown`'s mutual recursion is WS-P9**, no longer conditional: L4 measured the mutual half directly (the region aborts after the gauge and runs through frames; frames 1,006 against 1,007), which is the measurement §17.154 item 3 waited for. WS-P9 turns tail calls between members of a direct component into jumps into one merged loop with a dispatch parameter, in `sakura_core::opt::tail_calls` so both backends gain, mirrored in the LIR under §17.118's parity; it runs after WS-P2b lands because both edit `opt.rs`/`emit.rs`, and before M4-OPT measures the bar. Why: the twin design is the smallest thing that removes frames from the hot path without making the runtime re-entrant; a counter is cheaper than an argument about whether the double run matters. Rejected: a nested trampoline inside a running task (re-entrant `run`); rewriting the call at the gauge (a twin has no frame to rewrite into); folding WS-P9 into M4-OPT (it edits shared Core files other agents are in). Performance: none beyond the counter. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8, plan `performance-r1.md` §9 (WS-P9 decided). Impact: Opus (M4-OPT, WS-P9), ABI 1.2.0 (Opus, M4-OPT).
161. **The pull-based JS boundary and handle kinds 5–6 are endorsed; the `Sql.sqlite` task question was decided in §17.159 (5) (REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "W2"; plan §7 W2 items 8–9; round 12).** Decision: (1) **Nothing callable crosses the boundary.** A clause's closure arguments stay in the guest frame and cross as `Unit`; JS code that needs one answers `4` (session, argument index, unit flag, arguments) and the guest performs the call and resumes the JS continuation, as for an operation. A JS function passed to an operation crosses as a handle of kind `callback` (5); the guest wraps it in a native closure bound to the clause's handlers and calling it runs the function on the host through `js_callback`; the result is an opaque handle of kind `jsvalue` (6) that the guest only passes back, never decodes. Invariants that go with the endorsement: a kind-5 handle is retired when its clause's handler is destroyed (WS12 item 8), a kind-6 value is never inspected by the guest, and a failure a callback sends to its clause's context skips the clause's continuation (W2 pinned it in `llm_stream_anthropic`). ABI §8.2's import and handle-kind lists are updated to what is built in the 1.2.0 bump (§17.160 (4)); until then L4's and W2's additions beyond the list are the as-built state and `parity.rs` fails on an import the host lacks. (2) **`Sql.sqlite`'s lazy `Fs.root()`** runs in the clause's task on both backends; that is §17.159 (5), decided in this round's security entry and implemented under WS12 item 8, after which `tests/wasm.rs` compares the trace with task numbers. The W1 question (whether internal tasks count in §17.80's numbering) is closed by it: a runtime-internal drive spends no task number, whether or not it performs. Why: a boundary that moves closures would need the host to hold guest continuations, and the guest's ownership rules (§7) would then have to reach JS values; the pull form keeps every continuation in the guest, which is also what makes `Host.close()` sound. Rejected: marshalling closures to JS functions (ownership leaks); a second numbering scheme for internal tasks. Performance: one host round trip per callback call, already measured in W2's parity runs. Changed: §17.80 (pointer, internal tasks), ABI §8.2 (1.2.0). Impact: Opus (WS12 item 8, ABI 1.2.0).
162. **Defect message and site parity on WASM is required for M4 exit (WS-P5 deviation 7, L4 deviation 9, W2 deviation 13; round 12).** Question: a WASM contract defect carries no span and says ``contract `nonzero` violated`` where JS says ``precondition `nonzero` of positive violated``; `parity.rs` compares codes only; W1 item 7 left `sakura.debug` spans to `sakura_lir`. Decision: the `defect` record (§12.2) is a versioned contract an agent reads, and its `message` and site are the fields that tell an agent what to fix; code-only parity is not parity. **L5** (Opus, M4-LIR, after WS12 item 8): the LIR carries WS-P5's site table into the module (`sakura.debug`, ABI §12, which already reserves the section), WASM defect records render the same message as JS from the same kind, contract name and def name, and the record's site is the same `file:line:col`; `parity.rs` compares code, message and site, masking only a backend-named expectation. Static messages such as `Option.expect on None` versus the program's own `"boom"` are in scope: the message is the program's text (§17.159 (3) reasoning). Acceptance: the parity matrix (§3.7 row 4) stays green with the three fields compared on every fixture and example; `defect_*` fixtures' `.stdout` files need no backend variants afterwards. Why: parity that compares codes only would let the two backends drift in exactly the field agents act on; the site table exists since WS-P5, so the cost is a section and a renderer. Rejected: declaring message parity an M5 item (M4's acceptance says "identical stdout and effect traces", and a defect's stderr record is the one output an agent must read); comparing messages but not sites (half a contract). Performance: a custom section, no run-time cost; the message is rendered only when a defect is raised. Changed: §14.3 M4 acceptance (defect records identical), plan §3.7 row 4 wording, §3.8. Impact: Opus (L5), Sol (M4-TEST (b) parity matrix compares the three fields).
163. **§12.1 is trimmed to the CLI that exists, and the three WASM-only gaps are each placed (M4-DOCS; round 12).** Decision: (1) **`sakura build --release` is removed from §12.1.** §6.1's sentence that checks run in every build stands; there is no build mode that drops checks, and the only build-time switches are `--target`, `--emit`, `--layout`, `--manifest` and `--out-dir`. If an optimisation level ever exists it will be an RFC, because "fast by default" (§17.103) means the default is the fast one. (2) **`sakura mcp serve --tools a,b`** stays in §11.2 as planned, labelled so, scheduled after the first release and outside M4; `docs/mcp.md` and the MCP page already say "planned", and `compiler_info.features` must not advertise it (§17.141). (3) **`--manifest` on `--target wasm` is built** with V4b (§17.164): the manifest's statuses come from the same proof records and the `target` field says which profile the obligations were discharged for; a WASM build without it has no assurance record, which contradicts §17.76's purpose. (4) **`sakura test --contracts` on WASM is built** in M4-OPT, whose proved-check deletion needs a test that runs contract tests on both backends against the same proofs; its parity is a `parity.rs` test-mode fixture. (5) **`--record` stays JS-only through M4**, documented in one sentence on the testing page: recording is a development-time activity, the JS backend is always present, and the cassette a JS run writes is read by both backends. It moves to M5's list only if a WASM-only host appears. Why: a documented flag that errors is a lie to an agent, and each gap is either cheap in an item already scheduled or has a stated reason to wait. Rejected: implementing `--release` as a no-op (a flag that does nothing teaches agents to pass it); dropping `--manifest` on WASM (the manifest is the trust record); building `--record` on WASM now (no consumer). Performance: none. Changed: §6.1, §11.2, §12.1 (build row). Impact: Opus (V4b: WASM manifest; M4-OPT: `--contracts` on WASM), Astra (testing page sentence, MCP page unchanged).
164. **Verify: `unknown(resource_limit)` is a reusable result for `sakura verify`; the summary counts reused records under schema 1.1; `Entry.format` is the version field; V4b runs now (REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "V4"; plan §7 V4 items 1, 3, 6; round 12).** Decision: (1) **An `unknown` whose reason is `resource_limit` is cached and reused by `sakura verify`**, and only there: under §17.106 the `rlimit` binds deterministically for a given key (solver identity, options and limit are in the key, the clock is not), so re-solving reproduces the same `unknown` at the full limit, the most expensive query in the tree, on every run. `sakura build` never reads an `unknown` (it drops a check only on `proved`, §17.98), so nothing moves from "history" to "a reason to drop a check". A timeout `unknown`, if a solver ever reports one, stays uncached. §17.86 is amended with a pointer. (2) **The `summary` record of `sakura verify --json` gains `reused`** (the number of obligations whose record came from an authenticated entry) beside its counts, and the human summary prints `(n reused)`; this is the acceptance of plan row 9 made observable without a solver wrapper. Adding a field is a MINOR change to the JSON Lines contract: records written by `sakura verify` carry `"schema_version":"1.1"`, consumers ignore fields they do not know (§12.2's rule from this entry on), and `sakura check`'s records stay `1.0` until they change. (3) **`Entry.format` (WS12 item 5) closes the version-field question**: a proof cache entry whose `format` differs from the compiler's is "missing" and is solved again, as pre-V4 entries are today; no migration code is written for a cache. (4) **V4b** (the i64 replay through the LIR evaluator, deviation 6) is unblocked by L4 and runs now, in `sakura_cli/src/verify.rs` and `sakura_eval`, in parallel with WS12 item 8 (different crates), together with the WASM manifest (§17.163 (3)). Acceptance: a `disproved` i64 obligation is `confirmed` by replay on the LIR evaluator and the JS-profile replay is unchanged; `sakura build --target wasm --manifest` writes the manifest with `target: "wasm"`. Why: determinism is what makes a cache honest, and §17.106 bought it for `unknown` too; a count in the summary is a smaller contract change than a per-record flag and says what an agent wants to know. Rejected: a per-obligation `reused` flag (noise in every record); reusing `unknown` in `build` (nothing to reuse: build reads only `proved`); a cache migration. Performance: a tree with `unknown` obligations verifies in the time of its changed obligations. Changed: §17.86 (pointer), §12.1 (`sakura verify` row: `summary.reused`), §12.2 (schema 1.1 for verify records; unknown-field rule). Impact: Opus (V4b; `reused`; `unknown` reuse, each with its test), Astra (`agents/verify.mdx`: reuse sentence, the `reused` count).
165. **Eval pilot disposition: no diagnostic contract or language change; three bounded message improvements with independent reproducers; the next eval's harness changes; no paid run without a fresh key and the owner's approval (REQUESTS 2026-10-18 "DX5 pilot"; `evals/agent-dx/reports/2026-10-18-pilot.md`; round 12).** Reproducers (this round, a scratch `sakura new lib` project, never a task file): a pure `apply(f: fn() -> Int ! {Env})` calling `f()`; a pure caller of a `! {Fail<ParseErr>}` function; `Env.gett("HOME")`; `List.lenght([1])`; `List.map([1, 2])`; `Env.get("A", "B")`. Findings: **`V3001`** already carries a `cause` span naming the call that introduced the effect (`` `f` performs `Env` here ``), distinguishes an ordinary effect from a residual `Fail` in its fixes ("Handle the effect locally with `with`" against "Recover from the failure locally with `try`/`catch`"), and `explain V3001` names both repairs; the request's first half is built. **`V1002`** on an effect operation has a suggestion and a `machine_applicable` fix (`Env.gett` → `Env.get`), but the module-function form (`List.lenght`: "`List` has no function or variant `lenght` in its home module `std/list`") has no suggestion though it names the module. **`V2002`** names the callee's type and the two counts but not the callee or its module ("this call passes 2 arguments to a function of type `fn(String) -> Option<String> ! {Env}`, which takes 1") and leaves `expected`/`actual` null. Decision: (1) **No new code, no schema change, no semantics change**: codes are stable, and the pilot shows retrieval exhaustion, not a missing feature. (2) **Three message items** (Opus, small, one PR, a `tests/ui` fixture each; message text is not part of the versioned contract, the code, schema and span roles are): `V2002` names the callee and its home module and fills `expected`/`actual` with the two counts (`` `Env.get` (std/env) takes 1 argument, this call passes 2 ``; a lambda or local keeps today's type form); `V1002`'s module-function form gets the same did-you-mean and `machine_applicable` fix as the effect form when one candidate is within edit distance 2; `V3001`'s `with` fix names the effect's deterministic std handler constructor when its home module exports exactly one (`with Env.of(…)` instead of `with ?handler`), applicability still `incomplete`, and `explain V3001` adds the `Fail` paragraph (`try`/`catch`) it lacks. (3) **Harness changes before any next run** (Opus, DX5 runner; offline replay coverage first): record every lookup identifier including `doc`, with returned size and success; split structured compiler diagnostics (parsed `--json` records) from codes quoted in retrieved text; export remaining aggregate input and the refused next-request count per episode; a separately budgeted higher-input arm (60,000 aggregate input) paired against the 40,000 arm in the same design, failures retained; at least two order seeds. (4) **Paid runs**: the exhausted key is gone and the $5.80 remainder authorises nothing; a next run needs a fresh owner key, the owner's written approval of the worst-case dollars and the episode and token caps, under the $10 total of plan `agent-dx.md` §3 unless the owner raises it. Task authoring (Astra) continues offline; no task solution is pasted into a skill or an `explain`. Why: the pilot's hard failures are censored by input, and the one submitted HTTP candidate's codes are ordinary resolve/arity/effect errors whose messages can carry one more fact each at no contract cost. Rejected: a new "unhandled effect in callback" code (the `cause` span is that fact); widening the fix to insert an argument or guess an API; relaxing a frozen signature or permission to lift a score. Performance: none. Changed: plan `agent-dx.md` §1 DX5 row (next-run conditions), §4. Impact: Opus (messages, harness), Astra (report follow-up, tasks), owner (key, approval).
166. **Order for the rest of M4 and the M4 exit gate (round 12).** The release `v0.1.0-alpha.1` is gated on WS12 items 1–3 and 5–7 and the owner's dispatch; nothing below waits on it except DX8's plugin. In flight: WS-P2b (`emit.rs`), WS12 items 1/2/5/3/4/7 (Opus), WS12 item 6 landed (Sol), M4-TEST (b) (Sol). Then, in this order, each with its owner: (1) **WS-P9** (Opus) after WS-P2b lands (§17.160 (5)); (2) **WS12 item 8** (Opus) after item 7 lands, carrying §17.159 (5) and §17.161's invariants; (3) **V4b + WASM `--manifest` + `reused` + `unknown` reuse** (Opus) now, in parallel with (2); (4) **DX5 harness telemetry and the three message items** (Opus) now, small, in parallel; (5) **L5** defect message and site parity (Opus) after (2), with M4-TEST (b) comparing the three fields (Sol); (6) **M4-OPT** (Opus) after (1), (2) and (5): `--layout=niche` with the research/08 §6.2 nested-option corpus on both layouts, proved-check deletion with the predicate's computation gone unless WS-P5's bracket elision already removed it, `--contracts` on WASM, the `restarts` counter and ABI 1.2.0 (§17.160, §17.161), and the §17.118 bar or its stated carry (§17.160 (1)); (7) **the M4 envelope** (Opus WS-B: the `wasm` lane with Node and Wasmtime engines; Sol: toolchains and the nightly; Astra: numbers) after (6); (8) **the quiet-window bench and bless**: the JS close-out (plan `performance-r1.md` §5) is ready now, since WS-P5, WS-P7 and WS-P8 have landed, and runs the first time the owner idles the machine; the WASM envelope joins at the next quiet window after (7); Astra measures, the owner blesses, agents never bless (§17.153); (9) **DX8**: the plugin (Sol) after `v0.1.0-alpha.1` has shipped and DX1 has been stable for that release; the playground (Opus) after (6); Linguist when the usage threshold is real. **M4 exit gate** (the owner, or a delegated reviewer as §17.159 (1) did): the parity matrix green on `{js, wasm} × {ubuntu, macos}` comparing stdout, traces, test records and defect code, message and site; `sakura verify` reusing records with `reused` in its summary and a `disproved` i64 obligation confirmed by replay; WS12 items 4 and 8 landed; `--layout=niche` passing the nested-option corpus; the §17.109 M4 ceilings measured on the reference machine at a quiet window and recorded in `bench/deltas.md`, each miss carried to a named decision; the §17.118 bars met or carried under §17.160 (1); M4-DOCS rechecked against the CLI built from the exit commit. Research/12 (Astra) starts now with the parts that need no quiet-window numbers (§17.167). Why: (1), (2) and (5) touch files M4-OPT needs settled; (3) and (4) touch none of them; the quiet window is the owner's calendar, not an agent's. Rejected: starting M4-OPT before WS-P9 (both edit `opt.rs`); blessing on a loaded host; holding the envelope for DX8. Changed: plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.8 (the table), §3.7 dependency line (pointer). Impact: Opus, Sol, Astra as named; the owner (release dispatch, quiet window, bless, exit gate).
167. **M5 planning starts now: research/12 first, then five workstreams with acceptance bars; no M5 code before a design round reads research/12 (plan §3.7 row 11; round 12).** Decision: (1) **Research first.** Astra writes `research/12-m5-native.md` now; the parts that need quiet-window numbers (the `List` representation question of §17.152, the record representation of §17.138) are marked "pending the §17.166 (8) window" and filled when it has run. The document answers: a Cranelift survey against ABI 1.x (calling convention, the frame protocol and twins natively, `i64`/`f64` scalars, the generic word, host imports as native function pointers, WASI-free), the native build of `sakura_rt` (it is Rust; what in `wasm.rs` is WASM-specific), Perceus reuse over the LIR's `Dup`/`Drop` with a hit-rate instrumentation design, borrow inference over §17.149's owned facts, `W8001`'s rule (when a persistent update copies because the value is shared, and where to point), and platform B's targets (§17.157 (2)). (2) **Workstreams, in order, each with its bar** (`docs/plans/m5-native.md`, a skeleton now, filled by round 13): **N0** reuse and borrow instrumentation on WASM (`reuses`, `borrows` counters; hit rate on the paired `rbtree` workloads, §17.118), so M5's optimisations are measured against today's runtime before a native backend exists; **N1** the native runtime: `sakura_rt` built as a native static library with the ABI §8 host imports for the root handlers (Console, Clock, Random, Env, Fs) in Rust, `host_root_handlers` and the run-mode corpus passing natively from the LIR evaluator's harness; **N2** the Cranelift backend: LIR → Cranelift IR, frame protocol and twins, `sakura build --target native` behind a feature flag, the full parity corpus `{js, wasm, native}` with identical stdout, traces and defect records; **N3** Perceus reuse: constructor sites reuse a uniquely owned object of the same size class, `List.set` and record update in place when unique, hit rate reported in every benchmark record, `sieve`/`nbody`/`rbtree` deltas against §17.152's carried ceilings; **N4** borrow inference: parameters the LIR proves only read are passed borrowed, `dups`/`drops` on the benchmark record fall by the amount research/12 predicts, parity unchanged; **N5** `W8001` copy diagnostics: a warning at the update whose value is shared, with a `cause` span at the sharing site, positive and negative `tests/ui` fixtures, never firing where reuse succeeded. M5's exit is §14.3's line plus the §17.109 M5 envelope on platform A, native and WASM both green on the parity matrix. (3) Owners: Astra (research/12, measurement), Opus (N0–N5), Sol (toolchains for the native lane, CI for platform B when it starts). Why: M4 taught that the ABI document written first let two implementers start in parallel (ABI §0); research/12 is that document for M5, and N0 makes every later claim measurable. Rejected: starting Cranelift before the native runtime exists (nothing to link); Perceus before its hit-rate counter (no bar); a second IR for native (the LIR is the backend-independent IR, §17.118). Performance: the §17.109 M5 envelope. Changed: plan `m5-native.md` (new skeleton), plan `m3-completion-and-m4.md` §3.7 row 11 (research now). Impact: Astra, Opus, Sol.
168. **Road to 1.0: a provisional outline (round 12).** Recorded as `docs/plans/road-to-1.0.md`, labelled provisional in every heading; nothing in it is a date or a promise. Stages: (1) `v0.1.0-alpha.1` (channels §17.157; M3 closed); (2) M4 exit (§17.166) and `v0.1.0`, the deliberately scoped first non-alpha preview STABILITY.md names; (3) **the normative spec** in `docs/spec/`: grammar (§3), static semantics (types, rows, refinements, contracts), dynamic semantics (effects, handlers, defects, determinism), the diagnostic contract (§12.2 schemas, code stability), the ABI as a normative annex, each section with its conformance fixtures, written from the implementation and the `tests/semantics` corpus, and only text that is pinned by a fixture is normative; it starts after M4 exit, with the grammar and diagnostics first because agents consume them; (4) **stability guarantees** before any `1.0`: source compatibility within a major, diagnostic codes never reused, `--json` schemas versioned and additive within a major, the ABI MAJOR rule (§0), a deprecation path of at least one minor, an MSRV policy; (5) **packages** (plan §6 question 1, milestone P): MVS resolution, lockfile hashing, capability ceilings, a registry decision, after M5 unless a consumer appears; (6) **platforms** B and C (§17.157 (2)) each after the previous stage has shipped and been accepted on a fresh host; (7) **the evidence threshold** from real usage before `1.0`: at least three programs outside this repository maintained for one release cycle by someone other than the owner and the agents, each with a `sakura test` suite and a reported upgrade across a minor; the agent-DX suite at a stated pass rate under a stated budget on two independent model families; zero open soundness bugs in the checker or verifier for one release cycle; the benchmark envelope held at a quiet window for two consecutive releases. Why: 1.0 is a claim about other people's programs, and the only evidence for it is other people's programs. Rejected: dating the stages; a spec written before the implementation it describes is stable; a 1.0 without packages. Changed: plan `road-to-1.0.md` (new). Impact: the owner (every stage boundary is theirs).
